### Mangaluru

#### Monti Fest: Where flowers, faith & 1st harvest meet

**In short:** As Mangalorean Catholics prepare for September 8, Monti Fest—the Feast of the Nativity of the Blessed Virgin Mary—brings together prayer, family, children, the harvest and a renewed call to care for creation. For nine days children carry colourful flowers to churches for the Flower Offering Novena; Monte Mariano at Farangipet near Mangaluru is traditionally regarded as the birthplace of the celebration.

As Mangalorean Catholics prepare for September 8, Monti Fest—the Feast of the Nativity of the Blessed Virgin Mary—brings together prayer, family, children, the harvest and a renewed call to care for creation. For nine days children carry colourful flowers to churches for the Flower Offering Novena; Monte Mariano at Farangipet near Mangaluru is traditionally regarded as the birthplace of the celebration. The first corn of the season is blessed and shared, and Bishop Peter Paul Saldanha of Mangalore Diocese marks September 8 as Environment Day and Girl Child Day in the Diocese, urging respect for creation and water conservation in line with the Pope’s monthly intention.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Monti Fest: Where flowers, faith & 1st harvest meet
- **Told:** As Mangalorean Catholics prepare for September 8, Monti Fest—the Feast of the Nativity of the Blessed Virgin Mary—brings together prayer, family, children, the harvest and a renewed call to care for creation.
- **Purpose:** The first corn of the season is blessed and shared, and Bishop Peter Paul Saldanha of Mangalore Diocese marks September 8 as Environment Day and Girl Child Day in the Diocese, urging respect for creation and water conservation in line with the Pope’s monthly intention.

**Important points**
- The first corn of the season is blessed and shared, and Bishop Peter Paul Saldanha of Mangalore Diocese marks September 8 as Environment Day and Girl Child Day in the Diocese, urging respect for creation and water conservation in line with the Pope’s monthly intention.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.3_

#### ‘Go Sanmaan Abhiyan’ to launch third phase

**In short:** The third phase of the ‘Go Sanmaan Abhiyan’ campaign to promote cow protection and conservation will be inaugurated at Sri Gorakshanatha Temple in Kadri, Mangaluru, on September 6 at 11 am in the presence of prominent seers. The campaign committee said a signature collection drive aims to mobilise public support and raise awareness about cattle’s importance in Indian culture, appealing to devotees and cow-protection supporters to participate; State coordinator Jeevan Ji, Dakshina Pranta Goseva Pramukh Praveen Saralaya, division dairy-products marketing chief Ashok Ilanthil and Mangaluru division Goseva Pramukh Gangadhar are among the organisers.

#### Street vendors evicted in Hampankatta

**In short:** Mangaluru City Corporation officials launched an eviction drive on Thursday evening against street vendors who had illegally encroached footpaths in Hampankatta and surrounding areas, removing flowers and temporary structures and warning of stringent legal action against repeat encroachment. Dakshina Kannada Zilla Beedhibadhi Vyaparasthara Sangha Honorary President B K Imthiyaz condemned the drive, saying flower vendors from Hassan, Mandya and other districts had come for Krishnashtami and that the Street Vendors (Protection of Livelihood and Regulation of Street Vending) Act, 2014 permits vending during festivals.

#### ‘Police dept must make greater use of science, technology’

**In short:** Mangaluru Western Range Deputy Inspector General of Police Chetan R said the police department must make greater use of science and technology in maintaining law and order and detecting crimes, speaking at the valedictory of the 8th Mangaluru Western Range-level Police Duty Meet where he distributed prizes to competition winners. He stressed that dedicated performance by personnel from constables to senior officers was essential for a safe, peaceful society and the country’s progress.

#### Case filed against associate NCC officers of NITK over financial irregularities

**In short:** Surathkal Police registered a case against NITK Associate NCC Officers Prof P Johnson and Prof H Shivananda after an internal Comptroller and Auditor General audit found TA/DA claim bills were based on false documents; the duo were detained and an internal investigation is underway. NITK Director B Ravi said the NCC unit must uphold morality and integrity, and appointed Dr Mohan Lal Meena, Assistant Professor in Chemical Engineering, as NCC caretaker officer in place of the ANOs.

#### Sewing machines donated to Lady Goschen Hospital

**In short:** The Indian Red Cross Society, Dakshina Kannada District, donated three electric sewing machines to Government Lady Goschen Hospital on Thursday. Medical Superintendent Dr M R Durgaprasad said the machines will help restore surgical garments used by patients, doctors and nurses, and DK District IRCS Chairman Shantharam Shetty said the initiative would promote reuse of garments and indirectly strengthen government financial resources.

#### Proposal for reconstruction of Karambar bridge

**In short:** The PWD sent a ₹2 crore reconstruction proposal in March 2025 after a portion of the 1963 arch bridge sank on Wednesday on the MIA–Bajpe SH-67. A temporary earthen culvert with concrete pipes allows light vehicles while heavy vehicles use alternate roads; city–airport traffic is unaffected as the bridge lies beyond MIA.

#### New coconut milk residue-based food ingredient launched

**In short:** ICAR-CPCRI Kasaragod released Kalpa Composite Flour, mixing coconut milk residue with cereal and millet flours in three variants with a three-month shelf life and 10–25% CMR incorporation. Abhijnana Food Products in Puttur is commercializing Nitya brand millet and ragi cookies using the ingredient.

#### District level Teachers' Day programme to start early in Mangaluru tomorrow

**In short:** In-charge Minister U.T. Khader allowed a forenoon Teachers' Day programme despite Chief Secretary Shalini Rajneesh's circular for afternoon events due to the Prajaseva launch.

#### Railways to install double boom barrier at Pandeshwar Level Crossing

**In short:** Palakkad Division, pending a road-over-bridge, will install a double boom-barrier at Pandeshwar Level Crossing at about ₹2 crore; tenders are open and work is expected to be finalised within a fortnight and completed in six months. MCC and the DC offered to widen a 200 m road stretch to four lanes when Railways widens the crossing; MLA Vedavyasa Kamath and MP Brijesh Chowta pursued the issue.

### Coastal Karnataka

#### Fraudster absconding for 14 years arrested

**In short:** The North sub-division crime detection team at Moodabidri has arrested Srinivasa B R (48) of Baithadka in Jallur village, Sullia taluk, who had absconded for 14 years without appearing before court in a fraud case involving lacs of rupees. Police said he availed a vehicle loan of Rs 10 lac from Shriram Finance in 2009 to purchase a Telcon EX 70 vehicle but failed to pay instalments or produce the vehicle for inspection; a case was registered at Moodabidri police station in 2012 and the court issued a non-bailable warrant in CC No 526/2025.

The North sub-division crime detection team at Moodabidri has arrested Srinivasa B R (48) of Baithadka in Jallur village, Sullia taluk, who had absconded for 14 years without appearing before court in a fraud case involving lacs of rupees. Police said he availed a vehicle loan of Rs 10 lac from Shriram Finance in 2009 to purchase a Telcon EX 70 vehicle but failed to pay instalments or produce the vehicle for inspection; a case was registered at Moodabidri police station in 2012 and the court issued a non-bailable warrant in CC No 526/2025. He was apprehended near Baithadka Junction on September 2 and produced before court; another warrant in a cheque bounce case is pending against him.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Fraudster absconding for 14 years arrested
- **Told:** The North sub-division crime detection team at Moodabidri has arrested Srinivasa B R (48) of Baithadka in Jallur village, Sullia taluk, who had absconded for 14 years without appearing before court in a fraud case involving lacs of rupees.
- **Purpose:** He was apprehended near Baithadka Junction on September 2 and produced before court; another warrant in a cheque bounce case is pending against him.

**Important points**
- He was apprehended near Baithadka Junction on September 2 and produced before court.
- another warrant in a cheque bounce case is pending against him.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.3_

#### Udupi cops arrest Cong leader, 2 others for extortion

**In short:** Kota Police have arrested Congress leader Nagendra Puthran (37) of Gobbarabettu in Kotathattu village, P Sumanth Kundar (40) of Padukere in Parampalli and Sachin Shriyan (32) of Sasthana for allegedly threatening to kill a man and demanding money over bags of rice kept in his front yard under the State government’s Anna Bhagya scheme. The complainant said the three verbally abused him and threatened extortion and murder on Monday, August 31; a team led by Sub-Inspectors Praveen Kumar R and Mahantesh Jabagouda under DSP Belliyappa KU and Brahmavara Circle Inspector Gopikrishna arrested Sachin on September 1 and the other two on September 2, and all three were produced before court.

#### Udupi prepares for Krishna Janmashtami festivities

**In short:** Elaborate preparations are underway in Udupi for two-day Sri Krishna Janmashtami celebrations on September 4 and 5, with special pujas, a night arghya ceremony, a two-day tiger dance competition, and Vittlapindi on September 5 featuring traditional Mallayuddha at Shiruru Mutt and a golden chariot procession of a clay Krishna idol along Ratha Beedi. Organisers are preparing about 1.5 lakh chaklis and roughly 30,000 pieces each of five varieties of sweets using nearly 20 quintals of jaggery and seven quintals of sugar, with around 80 cooks involved; wrestlers from the Mysuru region have been invited, and Mutt Diwan Udayakumar Saralattaya said the mutt’s festival idol would not be taken out—instead a clay idol would be worshipped on September 4 and carried in procession the next day.

#### Bhatkal ASP cracks down on sand trucks

**In short:** Bhatkal Sub-Divisional Assistant Superintendent of Police Ranu Gupta, IPS, has launched a crackdown on illegal sand transportation soon after taking charge, seizing six tipper trucks and detaining six persons—Harish, Ajit, Anand, Prakash, Maruti and Nazir. Police sources said three trucks were seized within Manki Police Station limits and three in Murdeshwar Police Station limits under ASP Ranu Gupta’s leadership, with cases registered at both stations and further investigation underway.

#### International conference on Ayurveda from September 30

**In short:** Sri Dharmasthala Manjunatheshwara (SDM) College of Ayurveda, Hospital and Research Centre, Udupi, will organise KSHAMATVAM 2026, the sixth international conference on Translational Ayurveda In Autoimmune Disorders, from September 30 to October 1 at the Bhavaprakasha Auditorium, Udupi, with a pre-conference workshop on September 29. Under the theme ‘Translational Ayurveda in Autoimmune Disorders: Bridging Classical Wisdom with Global Healthcare’, the two-day programme will feature keynote lectures, scientific sessions, panel discussions, and paper and poster presentations, alongside an Ayurveda Expo showcasing pharmaceuticals, diagnostics, research and wellness services.

Under the theme ‘Translational Ayurveda in Autoimmune Disorders: Bridging Classical Wisdom with Global Healthcare’, the two-day programme will feature keynote lectures, scientific sessions, panel discussions, and paper and poster presentations, alongside an Ayurveda Expo showcasing pharmaceuticals, diagnostics, research and wellness services.

#### Khader to launch Praja Seva Andolana in Puttur tomorrow

**In short:** Dakshina Kannada District In-charge Minister U T Khader will launch the ‘Praja Seva Andolana’, Chief Minister D K Shivakumar’s initiative to take government to people’s doorsteps, at the Town hall in Puttur on September 5. Praja Seva Abhiyan meetings will be held at taluk and hobli levels across the state every Saturday, with AI-based outbound calls and Gram One kiosks at venues; grievances will be handled through separate new and unresolved queues, and eligible beneficiaries will receive on-the-spot sanction letters at meetings.

#### Banks told to ensure timely, adequate credit for eligible farmers

**In short:** Udupi Zilla Panchayat CEO Prateek Bayal told a district-level Lead Bank progress review meeting that banks must ensure eligible farmers receive timely and adequate credit, adopt a friendly approach to beneficiaries, and strengthen awareness on preventing financial fraud, including use of 1600-series numbers for transaction calls. As of June-end the district had 440 bank branches with deposits of Rs 48,715.93 crore and advances of Rs 24,184.84 crore, a credit-deposit ratio of 49.64% against the RBI minimum of 60%, and annual credit plan achievement of 39.41% (Rs 7,302.87 crore of Rs 18,532.26 crore).

#### Forest staff seek divine intervention to tackle elephant menace

**In short:** Belthangady forest department staff offered ‘Rangapooja’ at the Sowthadka Shree Mahaganapathi kshetra in Kokkada, praying that wild elephants would not cause loss of human life or damage to agriculture. During 2026-27, over 80 wild elephants destroyed crops and areca plantations worth over Rs 77 lakh in Belthangady, Sullia and Kadaba taluks; Rs 3.30 lakh compensation was released to 19 people while Rs 73 lakh remained pending for 381, and forest officials have submitted a proposal to establish an elephant camp at Gundya.

#### Three held for assault on student

**In short:** Moodbidri Police arrested three members of a Hindu organisation—Prem Bhandari, Sanjay Hegde and Ritil, all residents of Moodbidri—for allegedly assaulting a male engineering college student at a local hotel where he had gone for lunch with a woman student from a different community, after objecting to him placing his hand on her shoulder. All three accused were later released on bail, police sources said.

#### Childhood Cancer Awareness Month observed

**In short:** A ‘Memory Wall’ with smiling faces and stories of survivor children and their parents was unveiled during the ‘Wings of hope’ programme to observe Childhood Cancer Awareness Month at the Manipal Hospice and Respite Center auditorium in Havanje, organised by the department of Paediatric Oncology, KMC and Hospital. Nearly 65 childhood cancer survivors and their parents participated; MAHE COO Teaching Hospitals Dr Sudhakar Kantipudi stressed MAHE’s commitment to compassionate care, and actor Roopashree Vorkady was felicitated.

#### Udupi: Elaborate security for Krishna Janmastami, Vitla Pindi

**In short:** Udupi SP Hariram Shankar said security includes 2 DySPs, 6 inspectors, 25 sub-inspectors, 186 constables, 50 Home Guards, KSRP, DAR, firemen, ambulances, and 3 drones, plus 45 new CCTVs and fingerprint scanners. Parking is arranged at MGM College, Rajangana, and other sites, with Akka Pade deployed for crowd and women's safety on Friday and Saturday.

### Karnataka

#### K'taka govt blinks; Parks Bill 'buried in the tunnel'

**In short:** The Karnataka government has decided to withdraw the controversial amendment to the Karnataka Government Parks (Preservation) Act, which allowed a 5% diversion of park land for public utility use. Briefing reporters after a cabinet meeting on Thursday, September 3, Deputy Chief Minister G Parameshwara said the Bill would be withdrawn, placed in the public domain for discussion, and reintroduced in the next session with amendments; the government will also inform the Governor.

The Karnataka government has decided to withdraw the controversial amendment to the Karnataka Government Parks (Preservation) Act, which allowed a 5% diversion of park land for public utility use. Briefing reporters after a cabinet meeting on Thursday, September 3, Deputy Chief Minister G Parameshwara said the Bill would be withdrawn, placed in the public domain for discussion, and reintroduced in the next session with amendments; the government will also inform the Governor. The announcement came two days after Chief Minister DK Shivakumar said they would revisit the amendment amid massive public protests, notably in Lalbagh, where several acres could be diverted for the CM's tunnel road project if the amendment stood.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** K'taka govt blinks; Parks Bill 'buried in the tunnel'
- **Told:** The Karnataka government has decided to withdraw the controversial amendment to the Karnataka Government Parks (Preservation) Act, which allowed a 5% diversion of park land for public utility use.
- **Purpose:** The announcement came two days after Chief Minister DK Shivakumar said they would revisit the amendment amid massive public protests, notably in Lalbagh, where several acres could be diverted for the CM's tunnel road project if the amendment stood.

**Important points**
- The announcement came two days after Chief Minister DK Shivakumar said they would revisit the amendment amid massive public protests, notably in Lalbagh, where several acres could be diverted for the CM's tunnel road project if the amendment stood.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.1_

#### KPSC irregularities: K'taka govt suspends IAS officer facing probe

**In short:** The Karnataka government on Thursday suspended IAS officer Gyanendra Kumar Gangwar, who faces allegations connected to irregularities in the KPSC recruitment process, while he remains in CID custody and the Directorate of Enforcement separately investigates the case. During suspension he shall be paid subsistence allowance under Rule 4 of All India Service (Discipline and Appeal) Rules 1969 and may not leave headquarters without written State Government permission.

The Karnataka government on Thursday suspended IAS officer Gyanendra Kumar Gangwar, who faces allegations connected to irregularities in the KPSC recruitment process, while he remains in CID custody and the Directorate of Enforcement separately investigates the case. During suspension he shall be paid subsistence allowance under Rule 4 of All India Service (Discipline and Appeal) Rules 1969 and may not leave headquarters without written State Government permission. CID officials arrested Gangwar on August 29 over irregularities in the KPSC veterinary officers recruitment examination; he served as Controller of Examinations at KPSC from February 2024 to March 2026.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** KPSC irregularities: K'taka govt suspends IAS officer facing probe
- **Told:** The Karnataka government on Thursday suspended IAS officer Gyanendra Kumar Gangwar, who faces allegations connected to irregularities in the KPSC recruitment process, while he remains in CID custody and the Directorate of Enforcement separately investigates the case.
- **Purpose:** CID officials arrested Gangwar on August 29 over irregularities in the KPSC veterinary officers recruitment examination; he served as Controller of Examinations at KPSC from February 2024 to March 2026.

**Important points**
- CID officials arrested Gangwar on August 29 over irregularities in the KPSC veterinary officers recruitment examination.
- he served as Controller of Examinations at KPSC from February 2024 to March 2026.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.1; News Trail Mangaluru p.7; The Hindu Mangaluru p.3_

#### Kingpin among 3 more held in fake currency case

**In short:** Police have arrested three more people, including the alleged kingpin, in connection with the counterfeit currency printing racket uncovered at a house under construction in Shantilal Layout in Ramenagara, taking the total number of arrests in the case to 10. The arrested have been identified as Ashraf Ali, 35, from Chamarajanagar, and Manju, 37, and Nagaraj, 48, both from Kanakapura; police suspect Ashraf Ali of being the kingpin behind the fake currency racket.

Police have arrested three more people, including the alleged kingpin, in connection with the counterfeit currency printing racket uncovered at a house under construction in Shantilal Layout in Ramenagara, taking the total number of arrests in the case to 10. The arrested have been identified as Ashraf Ali, 35, from Chamarajanagar, and Manju, 37, and Nagaraj, 48, both from Kanakapura; police suspect Ashraf Ali of being the kingpin behind the fake currency racket. Earlier, on August 31, Ijoor police had arrested seven people during a raid on the house where counterfeit ₹200 and ₹500 denomination notes were allegedly being printed, and seized paper, chemicals and printing equipment.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Kingpin among 3 more held in fake currency case
- **Told:** Police have arrested three more people, including the alleged kingpin, in connection with the counterfeit currency printing racket uncovered at a house under construction in Shantilal Layout in Ramenagara, taking the total number of arrests in the case to 10.

#### K'taka targets 66 GW power capacity by 2030, puts clean energy at core

**In short:** Karnataka has unveiled a 10-year roadmap to raise its installed power capacity from over 39 GW currently to 66 GW by 2030, with the expansion expected to be overwhelmingly focused on clean energy, the Energy Department said on Thursday. Additional Chief Secretary, Energy Department, Gaurav Gupta outlined the roadmap at the Focus State Session for Karnataka during Bharat Electricity, POWER-GEN India and Indian Utility Week 2026 at Yashobhoomi in Bengaluru.

Karnataka has unveiled a 10-year roadmap to raise its installed power capacity from over 39 GW currently to 66 GW by 2030, with the expansion expected to be overwhelmingly focused on clean energy, the Energy Department said on Thursday. Additional Chief Secretary, Energy Department, Gaurav Gupta outlined the roadmap at the Focus State Session for Karnataka during Bharat Electricity, POWER-GEN India and Indian Utility Week 2026 at Yashobhoomi in Bengaluru. Electricity demand is expected to double over the next decade, and Karnataka expects to attract Rs two lakh crore to Rs three lakh crore in private investment in the power sector over the next four to five years while advancing projects including the 2,000 MW Sharavathi Pumped Storage Project and expansion of the Pavagada Solar Park.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** K'taka targets 66 GW power capacity by 2030, puts clean energy at core
- **Told:** Karnataka has unveiled a 10-year roadmap to raise its installed power capacity from over 39 GW currently to 66 GW by 2030, with the expansion expected to be overwhelmingly focused on clean energy, the Energy Department said on Thursday.
- **Purpose:** Electricity demand is expected to double over the next decade, and Karnataka expects to attract Rs two lakh crore to Rs three lakh crore in private investment in the power sector over the next four to five years while advancing projects including the 2,000 MW Sharavathi Pumped Storage Project and expansion of the Pavagada Solar Park.

**Important points**
- Electricity demand is expected to double over the next decade, and Karnataka expects to attract Rs two lakh crore to Rs three lakh crore in private investment in the power sector over the next four to five years while advancing projects including the 2,000 MW Sharavathi Pumped Storage Project and expansion of the Pavagada Solar Park.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.2_

#### Wipro to invest Rs 1k cr in hydraulic pump manufacturing plant

**In short:** Wipro Kawasaki Precision Machinery, a manufacturer of hydraulic pumps, has come forward to establish a manufacturing plant with an investment of Rs 1,000 crore in Karnataka, state Large and Medium Industries Minister M B Patil said on Thursday. The project, proposed to be set up in Phase V of the Harohalli Industrial Area, is expected to generate employment for around 1,200 people.

Wipro Kawasaki Precision Machinery, a manufacturer of hydraulic pumps, has come forward to establish a manufacturing plant with an investment of Rs 1,000 crore in Karnataka, state Large and Medium Industries Minister M B Patil said on Thursday. The project, proposed to be set up in Phase V of the Harohalli Industrial Area, is expected to generate employment for around 1,200 people. Japan-based Kawasaki, in partnership with Wipro, has sought 30 acres of land for the facility, with operations expected to commence within a year; the company currently manufactures 80,000 hydraulic pumps annually at a leased unit at Kumbalgodu.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Wipro to invest Rs 1k cr in hydraulic pump manufacturing plant
- **Told:** Wipro Kawasaki Precision Machinery, a manufacturer of hydraulic pumps, has come forward to establish a manufacturing plant with an investment of Rs 1,000 crore in Karnataka, state Large and Medium Industries Minister M B Patil said on Thursday.
- **Purpose:** Japan-based Kawasaki, in partnership with Wipro, has sought 30 acres of land for the facility, with operations expected to commence within a year; the company currently manufactures 80,000 hydraulic pumps annually at a leased unit at Kumbalgodu.

**Important points**
- Japan-based Kawasaki, in partnership with Wipro, has sought 30 acres of land for the facility, with operations expected to commence within a year.
- the company currently manufactures 80,000 hydraulic pumps annually at a leased unit at Kumbalgodu.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.2; News Trail Mangaluru p.12_

#### KAT stays suspension of two teachers over RSS march participation

**In short:** The Kalaburagi bench of the Karnataka Administrative Tribunal has stayed the suspension of government primary school teachers Gururaj Joshi of Hunasagi town and Anna Saheb Deshmukh of Kuppi school in Yadgir district, who were accused of participating in an RSS route march in Hunasagi on October 10, 2025, wearing the organisation’s uniform. Deputy Director Channabasappa Madhol had suspended them on August 20 following a government direction after complaints, including from Ambedkar Swabhimani Sena district president Jummanna Gudimani to ministers Priyank Kharge and Madhu Bangarappa; the teachers said the Sunday event was cultural and outside working hours, but the department held it a prima facie violation of Karnataka Civil Services (Conduct) Rules.

#### BSNL SIM cards for 67 rural post offices

**In short:** Financial and postal services in 67 rural post offices across Karnataka have been restored following intervention by Uttara Kannada MP Vishweshwar Hegde Kageri over a non-availability of BSNL SIM cards that had blocked India Post Payments Bank and other digital services for senior citizens, farmers, widows and persons with disabilities relying on postal banking. Of the affected offices, 43 were in Uttara Kannada, 22 in Shivamogga and two in Chikkamagaluru district; these locations had no private telecom network apart from BSNL, making the SIM shortage a major hurdle for pension and welfare transactions.

#### Kodavas reiterate demand for special constitutional status

**In short:** The Codava National Council reiterated its demand for special constitutional status and greater political autonomy for the Codava community at its 31st annual public Kailpoud celebrations at Capitol Village, Madikeri, on September 1. CNC president N.U.

The Codava National Council reiterated its demand for special constitutional status and greater political autonomy for the Codava community at its 31st annual public Kailpoud celebrations at Capitol Village, Madikeri, on September 1. CNC president N.U. Nachappa said the organisation would continue efforts for constitutional recognition and listed 10 demands including geopolitical autonomy for Codavaland, special ethnic classification, inclusion of Codava Thakk in the Eighth Schedule, protection of the traditional gun, UNESCO recognition of Codava heritage, an Inner Line Permit system, a separate legislative constituency and restoration of hereditary lands; Federation of New States vice-president Mettaramarao (IRS, VRS) said the Codavaland demand was among the oldest statehood claims and deserved priority.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Kodavas reiterate demand for special constitutional status
- **Told:** The Codava National Council reiterated its demand for special constitutional status and greater political autonomy for the Codava community at its 31st annual public Kailpoud celebrations at Capitol Village, Madikeri, on September 1.
- **Purpose:** Nachappa said the organisation would continue efforts for constitutional recognition and listed 10 demands including geopolitical autonomy for Codavaland, special ethnic classification, inclusion of Codava Thakk in the Eighth Schedule, protection of the traditional gun, UNESCO recognition of Codava heritage, an Inner Line Permit system, a separate legislative constituency and restoration of hereditary lands; Federation of New States vice-president Mettaramarao (IRS, VRS) said the Codavaland demand was among the oldest statehood claims and deserved priority.

**Important points**
- Nachappa said the organisation would continue efforts for constitutional recognition and listed 10 demands including geopolitical autonomy for Codavaland, special ethnic classification, inclusion of Codava Thakk in the Eighth Schedule, protection of the traditional gun.
- UNESCO recognition of Codava heritage, an Inner Line Permit system, a separate legislative constituency and restoration of hereditary lands.
- Federation of New States vice-president Mettaramarao (IRS, VRS) said the Codavaland demand was among the oldest statehood claims and deserved priority.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.3_

#### State to hold 3-day special session on drought, Kasturirangan report

**In short:** A three-day special session will be convened from September 21 to 23 to discuss the drought situation in Karnataka and the Kasturirangan Committee report. Deputy Chief Minister G.

A three-day special session will be convened from September 21 to 23 to discuss the drought situation in Karnataka and the Kasturirangan Committee report. Deputy Chief Minister G. Parameshwara said about 50 to 60 taluks may be added to the drought-hit list in a second round, ₹1,229 crore approved under the State Disaster Relief Fund is yet to be released though ₹187 crore has already been spent and ₹329 crore is with Deputy Commissioners, and Leader of the Opposition R. Ashok demanded at least a week-long session. The Cabinet also cleared Invest Karnataka 2027 from April 20, a ₹249 crore development plan for University of Visvesvaraya College of Engineering on the IIT model, and ₹46 crore for trauma centres including 100 basic life support ambulances.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** State to hold 3-day special session on drought, Kasturirangan report
- **Told:** A three-day special session will be convened from September 21 to 23 to discuss the drought situation in Karnataka and the Kasturirangan Committee report.
- **Purpose:** The Cabinet also cleared Invest Karnataka 2027 from April 20, a ₹249 crore development plan for University of Visvesvaraya College of Engineering on the IIT model, and ₹46 crore for trauma centres including 100 basic life support ambulances.

**Important points**
- Parameshwara said about 50 to 60 taluks may be added to the drought-hit list in a second round, ₹1,229 crore approved under the State Disaster Relief Fund is yet to be released though ₹187 crore has already been spent and ₹329 crore is with Deputy Commissioners, and Leader of the Opposition R.
- Ashok demanded at least a week-long session.
- The Cabinet also cleared Invest Karnataka 2027 from April 20, a ₹249 crore development plan for University of Visvesvaraya College of Engineering on the IIT model, and ₹46 crore for trauma centres including 100 basic life support ambulances.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.5; The Hindu Mangaluru p.6_

#### Min meets CWC chairman, seeks technical clearance for Mekedatu project

**In short:** Water Resources Minister N Chaluvarayaswamy held a high-level meeting with Central Water Commission Chairman Anupam Prasad in New Delhi on Wednesday to seek statutory and technical approvals for the Mekedatu Balancing Reservoir Project across the Cauvery river. The meeting focused on Karnataka's Comprehensive Compliance Report in response to CWC observations on the Detailed Project Report, following a recent site inspection by CWC Member Yogesh Paitankar; the minister underlined the project's importance for Bengaluru's long-term drinking water needs and requested the CWC to complete its technical examination at the earliest.

#### Bengaluru to host Invest K'taka-2027 from April 20 to 23 next year: Dy CM

**In short:** The Karnataka government will organise the global investors' meet Invest Karnataka 2027 in Bengaluru from April 20 to 23, Deputy Chief Minister G. Parameshwara said on Thursday after a cabinet meeting.

The Karnataka government will organise the global investors' meet Invest Karnataka 2027 in Bengaluru from April 20 to 23, Deputy Chief Minister G. Parameshwara said on Thursday after a cabinet meeting. Parameshwara said the event is aimed at attracting global capital and overseas investors, not domestic investment alone, and clarified it could be described as a four-day programme going by the dates; the announcement comes alongside the cabinet's decision to establish a Department of Global Investment and Overseas Affairs to facilitate foreign investment and provide a single-window mechanism for investors and NRIs.

#### Bail plea of MLA's daughter dismissed

**In short:** The anticipatory bail petition filed by Aishwarya, daughter of MLA Suresh Gowda, in a case involving the alleged assault of woman PSI Savitha has been dismissed by the Srirangapatna 3rd Additional District and Sessions Court. According to the complaint, Aishwarya allegedly slapped the police officer on August 12 at Arathi Ukkada in Srirangapatna taluk while Savitha was performing her duty, following which a case was registered at Kyathanahalli police station; with the plea rejected, police have stepped up efforts to locate Aishwarya and take her into custody.

#### No Balundagi canal water for 40 Afzalpur villages

**In short:** Farmers alleged that despite a month having passed since water was released into the Balundagi canal under the Sonna Bhima Lift Irrigation Sub-Division in Afzalpur taluk, tail-end villages among the 40 supposed to be supplied are not receiving even a drop of water. Farmer leaders and Karnataka Rakshana Vedike members began a road blockade on the Kalaburagi-Solapur National Highway near Atanur village around 9 a.m.

Farmers alleged that despite a month having passed since water was released into the Balundagi canal under the Sonna Bhima Lift Irrigation Sub-Division in Afzalpur taluk, tail-end villages among the 40 supposed to be supplied are not receiving even a drop of water. Farmer leaders and Karnataka Rakshana Vedike members began a road blockade on the Kalaburagi-Solapur National Highway near Atanur village around 9 a.m. on Thursday, alleging officials fail to monitor how far water reaches and that released water is wasted midway; Tahsildar Sanjeevkumar Dasar and police visited the site to persuade protesters to withdraw.

#### Fix sugarcane price, install fencing for farmers: AAP

**In short:** Aam Aadmi Party leaders staged a protest from Ambedkar Circle in Bidar to the Deputy Commissioner's office and submitted a memorandum to the Chief Minister through the DC, urging the government to fix sugarcane prices for district farmers at ₹4,000 to ₹4,500 per tonne and install fencing around farmers' fields to protect crops from wild animals entering from nearby forest areas. The leaders cited rising production costs, noted that sugarcane farmers in Punjab are paid ₹4,160 per tonne, and demanded a special scheme to protect Bidar farmers facing significant crop losses from frequent wild-animal incursions.

#### Jeweller's house burgled in Tumakuru, valuables worth Rs 2.25 crore stolen

**In short:** Gold and silver ornaments and other valuables worth around Rs 2.25 crore were stolen from jeweller Laksham Ganpat Pawar's house in Tumakuru when he and his family were away in Sangli, Maharashtra for his son's wedding on August 29 and 30, police said on Thursday. Thieves broke in after cutting the grill on the compound wall and windows, wore masks to avoid identification, and stole gold biscuits, silver bars, and silver coins.

#### BJP's 'Ballari Chalo' padayatra enters Sindhanur; Vijayendra raises farmers' issues

**In short:** The third day of the BJP's 'Ballari Chalo' padayatra, organised to highlight alleged administrative failures of the Congress government and problems faced by farmers and oppressed communities, concluded in Sindhanur on Thursday after covering about 20 km from Gudadoor. BJP state president B Y Vijayendra, who led the march from a bullock cart carrying a plough, demanded crop loans of drought-hit farmers be waived up to Rs 5 lakh, alleged diversion of Scheduled Caste and Tribal Sub-Plan funds, claimed 3,655 farmer suicides in Karnataka over the past three years including 937 in Kalyana Karnataka, and welcomed the government's decision to hold a special legislature session from September 21 to 23 while saying the BJP would raise issues including agricultural loan waiver during proceedings.

#### GBA report card: B'luru's civic overhaul yet to happen

**In short:** One year after the Greater Bengaluru Authority became operational on 2 September last year, splitting the erstwhile BBMP into five smaller corporations, public infrastructure experts say Bengaluru still has no elected council since September 2020 and its civic problems have compounded. Ashwin Mahesh said the GBA has not moved beyond its concept stage, Chief Commissioner Maheswara Rao cited West Asia war-related bitumen shortages for delays, and opposition leader R.

One year after the Greater Bengaluru Authority became operational on 2 September last year, splitting the erstwhile BBMP into five smaller corporations, public infrastructure experts say Bengaluru still has no elected council since September 2020 and its civic problems have compounded. Ashwin Mahesh said the GBA has not moved beyond its concept stage, Chief Commissioner Maheswara Rao cited West Asia war-related bitumen shortages for delays, and opposition leader R. Ashoka said Rs 2,199.99 crore in promised infrastructure support remains unfulfilled while major works have shifted to B-SMILE. Experts say the Shivakumar-led government has sought further election delays, neglected the outer periphery, and left little room in the treasury after guarantee schemes, landing the trouble at the doorstep of CM D.K. Shivakumar, who championed the GBA.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** GBA report card: B'luru's civic overhaul yet to happen
- **Told:** One year after the Greater Bengaluru Authority became operational on 2 September last year, splitting the erstwhile BBMP into five smaller corporations, public infrastructure experts say Bengaluru still has no elected council since September 2020 and its civic problems have compounded.
- **Purpose:** Shivakumar, who championed the GBA.

**Important points**
- Ashoka said Rs 2,199.99 crore in promised infrastructure support remains unfulfilled while major works have shifted to B-SMILE.
- Experts say the Shivakumar-led government has sought further election delays, neglected the outer periphery, and left little room in the treasury after guarantee schemes, landing the trouble at the doorstep of CM D.K.
- Shivakumar, who championed the GBA.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.6_

#### Rain deficit drives rise in crop insurance enrolment

**In short:** A severe monsoon deficit across Karnataka has driven a record surge in farmer enrolment for the Pradhan Mantri Fasal Bima Yojana, with kharif registrations nearly doubling from 20,91,575 in 2025 to 37,45,804 in 2026 as the last date for enrolment ended on August 31. Economically backward and drought-prone districts of Kalaburagi, Bidar, Haveri, Vijayapura, Gadag and Chitradurga account for half of total farmers who insured kharif crops, with around 23% coming from the tur-growing belt of Kalaburagi and Bidar.

A severe monsoon deficit across Karnataka has driven a record surge in farmer enrolment for the Pradhan Mantri Fasal Bima Yojana, with kharif registrations nearly doubling from 20,91,575 in 2025 to 37,45,804 in 2026 as the last date for enrolment ended on August 31. Economically backward and drought-prone districts of Kalaburagi, Bidar, Haveri, Vijayapura, Gadag and Chitradurga account for half of total farmers who insured kharif crops, with around 23% coming from the tur-growing belt of Kalaburagi and Bidar. Farmer leaders including Karnataka Prantha Raitha Sangha's Kalaburagi district president Sharanabasappa Mamashetty criticised the compensation formula and random yield assessment, noting the government and insurance companies have not yet disbursed 20% of the previous year's crop insurance compensation; Agriculture Department Director Dr G T Putra said the last kharif installment has been released and rabi processing initiated, citing awareness campaigns after rainfall deficit forecasts that yielded a record 49.5 lakh agriculture and horticulture farmer applications under Fasal Bima Yojana.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Rain deficit drives rise in crop insurance enrolment
- **Told:** A severe monsoon deficit across Karnataka has driven a record surge in farmer enrolment for the Pradhan Mantri Fasal Bima Yojana, with kharif registrations nearly doubling from 20,91,575 in 2025 to 37,45,804 in 2026 as the last date for enrolment ended on August 31.
- **Purpose:** Farmer leaders including Karnataka Prantha Raitha Sangha's Kalaburagi district president Sharanabasappa Mamashetty criticised the compensation formula and random yield assessment, noting the government and insurance companies have not yet disbursed 20% of the previous year's crop insurance compensation; Agriculture Department Director Dr G T Putra said the last kharif installment has been released and rabi processing initiated, citing awareness campaigns after rainfall deficit forecasts that yielded a record 49.5 lakh agriculture and horticulture farmer applications under Fasal Bima Yojana.

**Important points**
- Farmer leaders including Karnataka Prantha Raitha Sangha's Kalaburagi district president Sharanabasappa Mamashetty criticised the compensation formula and random yield assessment, noting the government and insurance companies have not yet disbursed 20% of the previous year's crop insurance compensation.
- Agriculture Department Director Dr G T Putra said the last kharif installment has been released and rabi processing initiated, citing awareness campaigns after rainfall deficit forecasts that yielded a record 49.5 lakh agriculture and horticulture farmer applications under Fasal Bima Yojana.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.1_

#### KIA plans home check-in, courier service

**In short:** Kempegowda International Airport is planning home check-in, courier and personal shopper services, with airport official Lewis saying passengers could send items left behind at security to their destination via a tracking mechanism and that courier and personal shopper services are likely in coming months while home check-in needs more time for legal requirements. Care by BLR currently offers meet-and-assist, baggage storage, porter assistance, valet parking, lounges and chauffeur-driven transport priced roughly between Rs 500 and Rs 7,000, with limousine services starting around Rs 4,500–Rs 5,000 and Rolls-Royce service about Rs 50,000 and above; about 24 kiosks operate across both terminals and the airport is considering extending services outside Bengaluru through tie-ups.

#### State okays dept on overseas affairs, global investments

**In short:** The Cabinet approved a department on overseas affairs and global investments hoped to foster global sister-city partnerships in mobility, urban governance, innovation, culture, museum and trade while connecting with global institutions for health, medicine and leisure tourism. A state facilitation council headed by the chief minister with the deputy CM as vice-chairman and 11 ministers will promote cross-sector linkages; for the first time a 10-member Group of Ministers headed by the CM—including G Parameshwara, K H Muniyappa, K J George, Priyank Kharge, Krishna Byre Gowda, Dr Yathindra Siddaramaiah, U T Khader, Laxman Savadi, Satish Jarkiholi and M B Patil—will scrutinise important policy decisions before Cabinet, and the Cabinet approved eight government spokespersons who will receive a daily morning briefing on important decisions and the official stand.

#### Bengaluru housing boom leaves Rs 40-lakh...

**In short:** Developers and analysts said Bengaluru's affordable housing benchmark needs updating as a Rs 40–45 lakh home in 2016 would cost roughly Rs 79–89 lakh today at 7% annual inflation, with soaring land and construction costs pushing developers toward properties priced at Rs 1.5 crore and above where average selling prices are close to Rs 10,000 per sq ft in most micro-markets. The share of Rs 40 lakh–Rs 80 lakh homes fell from 57% of sales in 2022 to 30% in 2023, 24% in 2024 and 7% in 2025 before marginally recovering to 8% in H1 2026, while the Rs 80 lakh–Rs 1.5 crore segment accounted for 30% in H1 2026; annual sales rose from 49,480 units in 2022 to 74,260 in 2025 with 31,720 in H1 2026, even as withdrawal of developer tax incentives under 80-IBA reduced cushion for affordable projects and achievable realisations below Rs 40 lakh are broadly limited to around Rs 4,000–Rs 4,200 per sq ft.

Developers and analysts said Bengaluru's affordable housing benchmark needs updating as a Rs 40–45 lakh home in 2016 would cost roughly Rs 79–89 lakh today at 7% annual inflation, with soaring land and construction costs pushing developers toward properties priced at Rs 1.5 crore and above where average selling prices are close to Rs 10,000 per sq ft in most micro-markets. The share of Rs 40 lakh–Rs 80 lakh homes fell from 57% of sales in 2022 to 30% in 2023, 24% in 2024 and 7% in 2025 before marginally recovering to 8% in H1 2026, while the Rs 80 lakh–Rs 1.5 crore segment accounted for 30% in H1 2026; annual sales rose from 49,480 units in 2022 to 74,260 in 2025 with 31,720 in H1 2026, even as withdrawal of developer tax incentives under 80-IBA reduced cushion for affordable projects and achievable realisations below Rs 40 lakh are broadly limited to around Rs 4,000–Rs 4,200 per sq ft.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Bengaluru housing boom leaves Rs 40-lakh
- **Told:** Developers and analysts said Bengaluru's affordable housing benchmark needs updating as a Rs 40–45 lakh home in 2016 would cost roughly Rs 79–89 lakh today at 7% annual inflation, with soaring land and construction costs pushing developers toward properties priced at Rs 1.5 crore and above where average selling prices are close to Rs 10,000 per sq ft in most micro-markets.
- **Purpose:** The share of Rs 40 lakh–Rs 80 lakh homes fell from 57% of sales in 2022 to 30% in 2023, 24% in 2024 and 7% in 2025 before marginally recovering to 8% in H1 2026, while the Rs 80 lakh–Rs 1.5 crore segment accounted for 30% in H1 2026; annual sales rose from 49,480 units in 2022 to 74,260 in 2025 with 31,720 in H1 2026, even as withdrawal of developer tax incentives under 80-IBA reduced cushion for affordable projects and achievable realisations below Rs 40 lakh are broadly limited to around Rs 4,000–Rs 4,200 per sq ft.

**Important points**
- annual sales rose from 49,480 units in 2022 to 74,260 in 2025 with 31,720 in H1 2026, even as withdrawal of developer tax incentives under 80-IBA reduced cushion for affordable projects and achievable realisations below Rs 40 lakh are broadly limited to around Rs 4,000–Rs 4,200 per sq ft.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.1; News Trail Mangaluru p.7_

#### As Modi plans for 2047, Karnataka sets targets for 2037

**In short:** While broadly aligning with Prime Minister Narendra Modi's Viksit Bharat 2047 vision, Congress-governed Karnataka has set 2037 as its deadline for goals including poverty eradication, with Chief Minister D K Shivakumar's Finance Advisor L K Atheeq saying the state will focus on Karnataka @ 2037 and interim targets for 2032—the next six years—in a consultative exercise also covering 2047. On Wednesday, NITI Aayog vice-chairperson Ashok Kumar Lahiri gave Shivakumar a presentation on envisioning 2047; the think tank flagged four macro challenges—regional imbalance beyond Bengaluru, a rural-urban per-capita income divide, unplanned urbanisation and climate resilience—and said Karnataka's strengths lie in IT, global capability centres, startups, aerospace, electronics and biotechnology.

While broadly aligning with Prime Minister Narendra Modi's Viksit Bharat 2047 vision, Congress-governed Karnataka has set 2037 as its deadline for goals including poverty eradication, with Chief Minister D K Shivakumar's Finance Advisor L K Atheeq saying the state will focus on Karnataka @ 2037 and interim targets for 2032—the next six years—in a consultative exercise also covering 2047. On Wednesday, NITI Aayog vice-chairperson Ashok Kumar Lahiri gave Shivakumar a presentation on envisioning 2047; the think tank flagged four macro challenges—regional imbalance beyond Bengaluru, a rural-urban per-capita income divide, unplanned urbanisation and climate resilience—and said Karnataka's strengths lie in IT, global capability centres, startups, aerospace, electronics and biotechnology. By 2037, Atheeq said Karnataka wants zero poverty, 100% completion of 12 years of schooling, infant mortality rate below 8, malnutrition below 10%, affordable healthcare, housing and higher education, at least 10 lakh jobs every year and balanced regional growth, banking on technology, high-tech manufacturing and export-oriented agriculture, horticulture and animal husbandry; nine states have already launched 2047 vision plans with NITI Aayog.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** As Modi plans for 2047, Karnataka sets targets for 2037
- **Told:** While broadly aligning with Prime Minister Narendra Modi's Viksit Bharat 2047 vision, Congress-governed Karnataka has set 2037 as its deadline for goals including poverty eradication, with Chief Minister D K Shivakumar's Finance Advisor L K Atheeq saying the state will focus on Karnataka @ 2037 and interim targets for 2032—the next six years—in a consultative exercise also covering 2047.
- **Purpose:** By 2037, Atheeq said Karnataka wants zero poverty, 100% completion of 12 years of schooling, infant mortality rate below 8, malnutrition below 10%, affordable healthcare, housing and higher education, at least 10 lakh jobs every year and balanced regional growth, banking on technology, high-tech manufacturing and export-oriented agriculture, horticulture and animal husbandry; nine states have already launched 2047 vision plans with NITI Aayog.

**Important points**
- By 2037.
- Atheeq said Karnataka wants zero poverty, 100% completion of 12 years of schooling, infant mortality rate below 8, malnutrition below 10%, affordable healthcare, housing and higher education, at least 10 lakh jobs every year and balanced regional growth, banking on technology, high-tech manufacturing and export-oriented agriculture, horticulture and animal husbandry.
- nine states have already launched 2047 vision plans with NITI Aayog.

#### Gen Z emerges as 'fifth estate' in Bengaluru youth dialogue

**In short:** The growing political awareness of Gen Z and its role in shaping India's democratic discourse came into focus at The GenZ Reckoning, a Salmaan Ahmed initiative in Bengaluru on Thursday that let young participants question political leaders on education, careers, leadership, culture, urban development and the future. The dialogue featured N A Haris, Dinesh Gundu Rao, Dr C N Ashwath Narayan, Ramya, Pawan Khera, Rizwan Arshad, Revathi Kamath, S R Vishwanath and Mohammed Haris Nalapad; Rao rejected the perception that young people lack political awareness and described citizen movements, youth-led NGOs and public participation as an emerging "fifth estate" that can address shortcomings in traditional democratic institutions, while cautioning against misinformation through unregulated social media echo chambers and calling for scientific temper.

The growing political awareness of Gen Z and its role in shaping India's democratic discourse came into focus at The GenZ Reckoning, a Salmaan Ahmed initiative in Bengaluru on Thursday that let young participants question political leaders on education, careers, leadership, culture, urban development and the future. The dialogue featured N A Haris, Dinesh Gundu Rao, Dr C N Ashwath Narayan, Ramya, Pawan Khera, Rizwan Arshad, Revathi Kamath, S R Vishwanath and Mohammed Haris Nalapad; Rao rejected the perception that young people lack political awareness and described citizen movements, youth-led NGOs and public participation as an emerging "fifth estate" that can address shortcomings in traditional democratic institutions, while cautioning against misinformation through unregulated social media echo chambers and calling for scientific temper. A student activist from Kolar raised the district's drinking water crisis, citing a 15-year struggle for clean water, seven FIRs while campaigning and health concerns linked to contaminated water; Kirthi Ganesh, NSUI Karnataka state president, acknowledged the seriousness of such concerns and stressed that sustained grassroots advocacy and institutional engagement were needed for long-term solutions.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Gen Z emerges as 'fifth estate' in Bengaluru youth dialogue
- **Told:** The growing political awareness of Gen Z and its role in shaping India's democratic discourse came into focus at The GenZ Reckoning, a Salmaan Ahmed initiative in Bengaluru on Thursday that let young participants question political leaders on education, careers, leadership, culture, urban development and the future.
- **Purpose:** A student activist from Kolar raised the district's drinking water crisis, citing a 15-year struggle for clean water, seven FIRs while campaigning and health concerns linked to contaminated water; Kirthi Ganesh, NSUI Karnataka state president, acknowledged the seriousness of such concerns and stressed that sustained grassroots advocacy and institutional engagement were needed for long-term solutions.

**Important points**
- A student activist from Kolar raised the district's drinking water crisis, citing a 15-year struggle for clean water, seven FIRs while campaigning and health concerns linked to contaminated water.
- Kirthi Ganesh, NSUI Karnataka state president, acknowledged the seriousness of such concerns and stressed that sustained grassroots advocacy and institutional engagement were needed for long-term solutions.

#### KPSC scam: CID conducts mahazar at suspect's Bengaluru house

**In short:** The Criminal Investigation Department (CID) on Thursday conducted a spot mahazar at the Sanjay Nagar, Bengaluru residential premises of Basavaraj Kannale, one of two key suspects in custody in the alleged Karnataka Public Service Commission (KPSC) irregularities. Kannale, described as the linchpin of the scam, allegedly orchestrated irregularities from Bengaluru and Bhalki, Bidar, with deals with candidates suspected to have been executed at the Sanjay Nagar house; the CID had previously searched it before his arrest.

The Criminal Investigation Department (CID) on Thursday conducted a spot mahazar at the Sanjay Nagar, Bengaluru residential premises of Basavaraj Kannale, one of two key suspects in custody in the alleged Karnataka Public Service Commission (KPSC) irregularities. Kannale, described as the linchpin of the scam, allegedly orchestrated irregularities from Bengaluru and Bhalki, Bidar, with deals with candidates suspected to have been executed at the Sanjay Nagar house; the CID had previously searched it before his arrest. This followed a Wednesday mahazar at the OMR strong room and KPSC office, and document searches at Om Lakshmi Souharda Sahakari Sangha Niyamitha in Bhalki where Kannale held accounts in relatives' names to launder proceeds; ex-KPSC chairman Shivashankarappa S Sahukar may be summoned again, while IAS officer Gyanendra Kumar Gangwar remains under interrogation over alleged irregularities in the Veterinary Officers' Recruitment Exam final selection.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** KPSC scam: CID conducts mahazar at suspect's Bengaluru house
- **Told:** The Criminal Investigation Department (CID) on Thursday conducted a spot mahazar at the Sanjay Nagar, Bengaluru residential premises of Basavaraj Kannale, one of two key suspects in custody in the alleged Karnataka Public Service Commission (KPSC) irregularities.
- **Purpose:** This followed a Wednesday mahazar at the OMR strong room and KPSC office, and document searches at Om Lakshmi Souharda Sahakari Sangha Niyamitha in Bhalki where Kannale held accounts in relatives' names to launder proceeds; ex-KPSC chairman Shivashankarappa S Sahukar may be summoned again, while IAS officer Gyanendra Kumar Gangwar remains under interrogation over alleged irregularities in the Veterinary Officers' Recruitment Exam final selection.

**Important points**

_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.6_

#### Protest against river linking projects gathers momentum in U-K

**In short:** The Bedti-Varada and Aghanashini-Vedavati river linking projects have drawn intense opposition from local communities and environmentalists, with Save Gangavali-Karavali awareness campaigns under the Bedti-Aghanashini basin conservation committee held at Gokarna, Karwar and Ankola on Thursday. More than 10,000 letters to Chief Minister demanding the projects be dropped were submitted to Deputy Commissioner K Lakshmipriya; committee president Ananth Hegde Ashishara said expert reports show low water flow in Bedti and Aghanashini rivers and warned the projects could adversely impact Kadamba Naval Base.

The Bedti-Varada and Aghanashini-Vedavati river linking projects have drawn intense opposition from local communities and environmentalists, with Save Gangavali-Karavali awareness campaigns under the Bedti-Aghanashini basin conservation committee held at Gokarna, Karwar and Ankola on Thursday. More than 10,000 letters to Chief Minister demanding the projects be dropped were submitted to Deputy Commissioner K Lakshmipriya; committee president Ananth Hegde Ashishara said expert reports show low water flow in Bedti and Aghanashini rivers and warned the projects could adversely impact Kadamba Naval Base. Former minister Anand Asnotikar, BJP state vice-president Rupali Naik and others attended as Gokarna saw a protest march; the Bedti-Varada project proposes diverting surplus west-flowing Bedti water to the east-flowing Varada to supply drinking water to arid districts including Haveri.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Protest against river linking projects gathers momentum in U-K
- **Told:** The Bedti-Varada and Aghanashini-Vedavati river linking projects have drawn intense opposition from local communities and environmentalists, with Save Gangavali-Karavali awareness campaigns under the Bedti-Aghanashini basin conservation committee held at Gokarna, Karwar and Ankola on Thursday.
- **Purpose:** Former minister Anand Asnotikar, BJP state vice-president Rupali Naik and others attended as Gokarna saw a protest march; the Bedti-Varada project proposes diverting surplus west-flowing Bedti water to the east-flowing Varada to supply drinking water to arid districts including Haveri.

**Important points**
- Former minister Anand Asnotikar, BJP state vice-president Rupali Naik and others attended as Gokarna saw a protest march.
- the Bedti-Varada project proposes diverting surplus west-flowing Bedti water to the east-flowing Varada to supply drinking water to arid districts including Haveri.

#### B'luru economy at $400 bn by 2037: Five growth drivers identified

**In short:** An Economic Master Plan draft presented to Chief Minister D K Shivakumar on Wednesday aims to transform the Bengaluru Metropolitan Region (BMR) into a $400-billion economy by 2037, when Bengaluru marks 500 years as a city, with 2–2.5 million jobs envisaged under a plan prepared with ISEG Foundation as knowledge partner. Five growth drivers are identified: technology, next-generation manufacturing, healthcare and education, infrastructure and sustainability, and planned urbanisation; IT, GCCs, AI and startups are flagged as biggest contributors, with proposals including dedicated IT/GCC hubs, deep-tech sandboxes, a dedicated AI mission, raising public transport modal share from 35% to 75% via Metro, suburban rail and RRTS, a second airport, ring/radial/chord roads, water augmentation and reuse, planned cities, affordable housing and slum rehabilitation.

An Economic Master Plan draft presented to Chief Minister D K Shivakumar on Wednesday aims to transform the Bengaluru Metropolitan Region (BMR) into a $400-billion economy by 2037, when Bengaluru marks 500 years as a city, with 2–2.5 million jobs envisaged under a plan prepared with ISEG Foundation as knowledge partner. Five growth drivers are identified: technology, next-generation manufacturing, healthcare and education, infrastructure and sustainability, and planned urbanisation; IT, GCCs, AI and startups are flagged as biggest contributors, with proposals including dedicated IT/GCC hubs, deep-tech sandboxes, a dedicated AI mission, raising public transport modal share from 35% to 75% via Metro, suburban rail and RRTS, a second airport, ring/radial/chord roads, water augmentation and reuse, planned cities, affordable housing and slum rehabilitation. The BMR is currently a $149-billion economy across three districts, with per capita income nearly twice the state average.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** B'luru economy at $400 bn by 2037: Five growth drivers identified
- **Told:** An Economic Master Plan draft presented to Chief Minister D K Shivakumar on Wednesday aims to transform the Bengaluru Metropolitan Region (BMR) into a $400-billion economy by 2037, when Bengaluru marks 500 years as a city, with 2–2.5 million jobs envisaged under a plan prepared with ISEG Foundation as knowledge partner.
- **Purpose:** The BMR is currently a $149-billion economy across three districts, with per capita income nearly twice the state average.

**Important points**

#### HC notice to ECI on appointing anganwadi workers as BLOs

**In short:** The High Court of Karnataka on Thursday ordered notice to the Election Commission of India (ECI), the Chief Electoral Officer, Karnataka and the state government in a PIL by the Karnataka State Anganwadi Workers and Helpers Union, State Committee, Davangere, against appointing anganwadi workers as Booth Level Officers (BLOs) in the Special Intensive Revision (SIR) of electoral rolls unless non-availability of eligible regular state or local government employees is recorded. A division bench of Chief Justice Vibhu Bakhru and Justice K S Hemalekha posted the matter to December 1; the petitioners cited ECI guidelines dated June 5, 2025 requiring BLOs first from Group C and above regular employees, with anganwadi workers only as a fallback subject to Non-Availability Certificate compliance, and sought review and substitution of existing anganwadi BLO deployments while stressing the PIL does not seek to obstruct elections.

#### Cabinet decides to withdraw contentious Parks Bill

**In short:** The Cabinet on Thursday decided to withdraw the contentious Parks bill (Karnataka Government Parks (Preservation) (Amendment) Act, 2026) by means of an ordinance, following backlash from citizens and Opposition parties. The bill, passed in the monsoon session, had amended the 1975 Act governing Bengaluru's lung spaces including Cubbon Park and Lalbagh to allow 5% park land for public purpose; Deputy Chief Minister G Parameshwara said it would be reintroduced in the next full-fledged session after modifications based on suggestions, with contentious clauses related to sale, lease, gift and mortgage to be reviewed after public consultations.

The Cabinet on Thursday decided to withdraw the contentious Parks bill (Karnataka Government Parks (Preservation) (Amendment) Act, 2026) by means of an ordinance, following backlash from citizens and Opposition parties. The bill, passed in the monsoon session, had amended the 1975 Act governing Bengaluru's lung spaces including Cubbon Park and Lalbagh to allow 5% park land for public purpose; Deputy Chief Minister G Parameshwara said it would be reintroduced in the next full-fledged session after modifications based on suggestions, with contentious clauses related to sale, lease, gift and mortgage to be reviewed after public consultations. The Cabinet withdrew the bill after discussing acquisition of land in Lalbagh, Bengaluru Palace and Cubbon Park for the tunnel road project; Chief Minister D K Shivakumar posted on X that the decision reflected the government's commitment to respect public opinion and reintroduce the Bill with necessary amendments.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Cabinet decides to withdraw contentious Parks Bill
- **Told:** The Cabinet on Thursday decided to withdraw the contentious Parks bill (Karnataka Government Parks (Preservation) (Amendment) Act, 2026) by means of an ordinance, following backlash from citizens and Opposition parties.
- **Purpose:** The Cabinet withdrew the bill after discussing acquisition of land in Lalbagh, Bengaluru Palace and Cubbon Park for the tunnel road project; Chief Minister D K Shivakumar posted on X that the decision reflected the government's commitment to respect public opinion and reintroduce the Bill with necessary amendments.

**Important points**
- The Cabinet withdrew the bill after discussing acquisition of land in Lalbagh, Bengaluru Palace and Cubbon Park for the tunnel road project.
- Chief Minister D K Shivakumar posted on X that the decision reflected the government's commitment to respect public opinion and reintroduce the Bill with necessary amendments.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.7; The Hindu Mangaluru p.3_

#### State BJP chief demands farm loan waiver up to Rs 5L

**In short:** Karnataka BJP president B Y Vijayendra has urged the state government to waive crop loans up to Rs 5 lakh, addressing a press conference after concluding the third day of the 'Ballari Chalo' padayatra in Sindhanur on Thursday. He demanded a special legislature session to discuss farmers' problems due to rainfall deficit, said the BJP government under B S Yediyurappa had released Rs 14 crore for the Navali balancing reservoir to address 30 tmcft silt accumulation in Tungabhadra reservoir while the current government had taken no measures, and cited 3,655 farmer suicides since Congress came to power—including 936 in Kalyana Karnataka in three years, with 294 in Kalaburagi, 220 in Bidar, 160 in Raichur and more than 80 in Koppal—and pending dues of Rs 145 crore to farmers for white jowar procurement.

#### Cabinet panel discusses UPSC model to end KPSC woes

**In short:** The first meeting of the Cabinet sub-committee on student issues and exam reforms was held on Thursday, deciding to collect opinions from experts including former vice chancellors before meeting stakeholders including students and parents. School Education Minister Madhu Bangarappa suggested proper publicity and a separate website for public suggestions; Medical Education Minister Dr Sharanprakash Patil said no decision was taken at the first meeting and members also deliberated on the KPSC issue, with some suggesting adoption of the Union Public Service Commission model and senior ministers expressing displeasure over KPSC's failure to conduct fair exams.

The first meeting of the Cabinet sub-committee on student issues and exam reforms was held on Thursday, deciding to collect opinions from experts including former vice chancellors before meeting stakeholders including students and parents. School Education Minister Madhu Bangarappa suggested proper publicity and a separate website for public suggestions; Medical Education Minister Dr Sharanprakash Patil said no decision was taken at the first meeting and members also deliberated on the KPSC issue, with some suggesting adoption of the Union Public Service Commission model and senior ministers expressing displeasure over KPSC's failure to conduct fair exams. The committee is headed by Health Minister U T Khader with Home Minister Priyank Kharge and Higher Education Minister Basavaraj Rayareddi as members, and has one month's time to submit its report after wider consultations.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Cabinet panel discusses UPSC model to end KPSC woes
- **Told:** The first meeting of the Cabinet sub-committee on student issues and exam reforms was held on Thursday, deciding to collect opinions from experts including former vice chancellors before meeting stakeholders including students and parents.
- **Purpose:** The committee is headed by Health Minister U T Khader with Home Minister Priyank Kharge and Higher Education Minister Basavaraj Rayareddi as members, and has one month's time to submit its report after wider consultations.

**Important points**

#### Special session from Sep 21-23

**In short:** A special session of the legislature will be convened from September 21 to 23 exclusively to discuss the deepening agrarian crisis and the Dr Kasturirangan report on the Western Ghats. Deputy Chief Minister G Parameshwara said this year is seeing the severest drought in 150 years due to super El Nino, impacting farmers, the state's economy and food security, that there was no opportunity to discuss this in the last session, and that although the government has rejected the Kasturirangan report many times it is coming up for discussion frequently and the government wants to discuss it in detail.

#### First Nagendra, now Parks Bill: BJP claims two victories

**In short:** Leader of the Opposition R Ashoka on Thursday termed the Karnataka Cabinet's decision to withdraw the Bill allowing 5% of park land to be used for infrastructure projects a victory for the BJP's 'Save Parks, Save Bengaluru' campaign. Ashoka said the BJP recorded two victories in a month—last week Bellary MLA B Nagendra resigned from Shivakumar's Cabinet over the Karnataka Maharshi Valmiki ST Development Corporation scam following BJP agitation—and charged the Bill would have been disastrous for future generations, that the 1975 Government Parks (Preservation) Act was meant to preserve green cover while flooding has increased in areas such as HSR Layout, Jayanagar and Hebbal, and that the government wanted to destroy parks to facilitate a tunnel road project for industrialist Gautam Adani; he said the BJP would oppose any future attempt to divert park land and had been preparing protests targeting five lakh signatures.

Leader of the Opposition R Ashoka on Thursday termed the Karnataka Cabinet's decision to withdraw the Bill allowing 5% of park land to be used for infrastructure projects a victory for the BJP's 'Save Parks, Save Bengaluru' campaign. #### Cabinet clears sites for 1,453 BMIC land losers in Bidadi: DCM

**In short:** The Karnataka cabinet on Thursday decided to allot sites to 1,453 farmers who parted with their land for the Bengaluru-Mysore Infrastructure Corridor (BMIC) project in the Bidadi sector, Deputy Chief Minister G Parameshwara said after the Cabinet sub-committee on BMIC held eight meetings. Issues relating to site distribution in Sector-A Bidadi have been mostly resolved while toll collection, townships, intersection and link road will be tackled in phases; with only 574 sites available (231 of 30x40 and 243 of 40x60) for 1,453 land losers, another 879 sites will be created across Varahasandra, Madavara, Kodigenahalli and Srikantapura Linganandanahalli with NICE providing land within two months, and the government on humanitarian consideration decided to give all sites even if not mentioned in the agreement following the Supreme Court's direction that the state could decide in Cabinet.

#### Other decisions

**In short:** The Cabinet approved Invest Karnataka summit 2027 from April 20–23; a steering committee headed by the CM to approve action plans prepared by district commissioners under Centre's district mineral foundation rules; permission for Ballari city corporation to install a Maharshi Valmiki statue in a public place; extension of a Rs 2,500 crore government guarantee for KASCARD bank for NABARD refinance for one year; approval of a modified UDAN regional connectivity scheme MoU with the Airports Authority of India and Centre for tier-II and tier-III cities; Rs 249 crore to upgrade Visvesvaraya Technological University on IIT lines; Rs 115 crore Byadgi-Guddadamallapura lift irrigation in Haveri to fill 137 lakes; Rs 137.82 crore Maddur road widening; and Rs 45 crore for comprehensive trauma centres in district and taluk hospitals plus 100 basic life support ambulances.

#### Shikhar, Mohsin script stunning recovery for Prez XI

**In short:** Tail-enders Shikhar Shetty (92 off 143 balls) and Mohsin Khan (51 not out off 50) forged a 101-run unbroken tenth-wicket stand as KSCA President's XI rallied from 295/9 to 396 all out to take the first-innings lead against Goa in the Dr (Capt) K Thimmappiah Memorial tournament at M Chinnaswamy Stadium on Thursday, leaving Goa reeling at 167/8 at stumps. At SDNRW Ground, Mysuru, KSCA Combined XI lost by an innings and 18 runs to Jharkhand after being bowled out for 66 and 273.

#### Karnataka's Sindhushree, Supriya in Asian Games squad

**In short:** Women's pole vault national record holder Sindhushree G and high jumper Supriya B are among six Karnataka track and field athletes in the 77-member Indian squad for the Asian Games in Aichi-Nagoya from September 19 to October 4, with Supriya having cleared 1.80m at the National Inter-State Championships and won gold at the World Athletics Continental Tour Silver Meet in Bhubaneswar. Neeraj Chopra was replaced by Yashvir Singh after pulling out with an ankle ligament tear, while Abhishek Pal was omitted after a prohibited substance was found in his dope sample.

#### KBA to host 3 big events beginning with South Zone meet from tomorrow

**In short:** The Karnataka Badminton Association will host three major tournaments, starting with the 80th South Zone Inter-State Badminton Championship in Bengaluru from September 5 to 8 — the event's return to the city after 23 years — followed by the inaugural KBA Diamond Jubilee India International Challenge from October 27 to November 1 with USD 25,000 prize money and the Junior Nationals in early December. Former national champion Raghu M and women's doubles pair Ashwini Bhat and Shikha Gautam will spearhead Karnataka's 34-member squad.

#### Nupura makes record sprint

**In short:** Karnataka's 100m hurdler Nupura Holla clocked 13.78 seconds to win gold and break the 14-year-old meet record of 14.06 at the National Youth Athletics Championships in Ludhiana on Thursday, coming close to the U-18 national record of 13.70. Karnataka finished third on the medals tally with three gold and two silver, and javelin thrower Pushpak Nelwade took silver with 76.09m.

#### 4,500 electric buses for Bengaluru

**In short:** The Cabinet cleared the Bengaluru Metropolitan Transport Corporation's proposal to add 4,500 electric buses over the next couple of years, a Union government-funded purchase at ₹1.2 crore to ₹1.5 crore per bus that significantly expands bus services in Bengaluru. The city already has the largest city bus fleet in the country, with around 7,000 buses.

#### Lokayukta registers suo motu case over ceiling collapse of school

**In short:** The Lokayukta registered a suo motu case over the collapse of a concrete ceiling at a government primary school in Mugalolli Tanda, Bagalkot district, during class hours, injuring six students whose injuries were reported minor. It directed random inspections of government primary schools across Bagalkot and a district-wide safety audit covering structural condition, hygiene, toilets and drinking water, and Leader of the Opposition R.

The Lokayukta registered a suo motu case over the collapse of a concrete ceiling at a government primary school in Mugalolli Tanda, Bagalkot district, during class hours, injuring six students whose injuries were reported minor. It directed random inspections of government primary schools across Bagalkot and a district-wide safety audit covering structural condition, hygiene, toilets and drinking water, and Leader of the Opposition R. Ashok targeted Congress leader Rahul Gandhi's 'Chhatron Ki Goonj' initiative on social media, demanding he visit the school and answer for unsafe classrooms in Congress-ruled Karnataka.

#### Cabinet approves inauguration of Maharshi Valmiki statue in Ballari

**In short:** The Karnataka Cabinet approved inauguration of the Maharshi Valmiki statue at S.P. Circle in Ballari city after the event remained stalled for nearly eight months following a January 1, 2026 clash between supporters of Ballari City MLA Nara Bharath Reddy and Gangavathi MLA Gali Janardhan Reddy in which a Congress supporter was killed and several were injured.

The Karnataka Cabinet approved inauguration of the Maharshi Valmiki statue at S.P. Circle in Ballari city after the event remained stalled for nearly eight months following a January 1, 2026 clash between supporters of Ballari City MLA Nara Bharath Reddy and Gangavathi MLA Gali Janardhan Reddy in which a Congress supporter was killed and several were injured. Carved in stone by sculptor Arun Yogiraj, the statue was originally scheduled for inauguration on January 3 but had remained covered with a saffron cloth since the clash.

Shivakumar, with Madhugiri MLA and former Minister K.N. Rajanna and MLC S. Ravi seen as frontrunners backed by Siddaramaiah and Shivakumar respectively. #### Eviction fear grips many Bengal migrant workers in Bengaluru

**In short:** Operation Mukta, aimed at identifying and tracking illegal immigrants in Bengaluru, has left nearly 400 migrant workers from West Bengal in and around Hulimangala in Electronics City anxious after police served show-cause notices on landlords of premises allegedly used for unauthorised garbage segregation citing health and sanitation concerns. Workers who have lived in the city for years as garbage segregators said they were not involved in any illegal activity and appealed to authorities to distinguish between illegal immigrants and genuine migrant labourers.

**In short:** Skipper Kafeel Ahmed scored 109 (107b, 19 fours) and took 3/42 as Jaihind CC B beat RBNCC B by three wickets in the KSCA Mysore Zone Sri Gopalaswamy Memorial tournament in Mysuru on Thursday, chasing down 203/7 after RBN-B were bowled out for 199 in 43.4 overs.
