### India

#### SC: Bar council can't punish law students

**In short:** The Supreme Court ruled Thursday that the Bar Council of India and state bar councils lack statutory authority to take disciplinary action against law students, holding such powers arise only after enrolment as advocates. A bench of CJI Surya Kant and Justices Joymalya Bagchi and V Mohana clarified disciplinary control over students rests solely with their parent institutions or authorities under institutional regulations and byelaws.

The Supreme Court ruled Thursday that the Bar Council of India and state bar councils lack statutory authority to take disciplinary action against law students, holding such powers arise only after enrolment as advocates. A bench of CJI Surya Kant and Justices Joymalya Bagchi and V Mohana clarified disciplinary control over students rests solely with their parent institutions or authorities under institutional regulations and byelaws. The court was hearing a writ petition by NALSAR alumni Mihira Sood and Abhishek Tiwari challenging BCI chairman Manan Kumar Mishra's August 13 directions to bar NALSAR's 2026 graduating batch from enrolment and order an inquiry over a campaign against the CJI; the BCI later withdrew those directions after public backlash. The bench declared the August 13 communications and any subsequent modified versions were "without any authority of law," made absolute its earlier order protecting NALSAR students and faculty from punitive action by the BCI or state bar councils, and disposed of the petition.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** SC: Bar council can't punish law students
- **Told:** The Supreme Court ruled Thursday that the Bar Council of India and state bar councils lack statutory authority to take disciplinary action against law students, holding such powers arise only after enrolment as advocates.
- **Purpose:** The bench declared the August 13 communications and any subsequent modified versions were "without any authority of law," made absolute its earlier order protecting NALSAR students and faculty from punitive action by the BCI or state bar councils, and disposed of the petition.

**Important points**
- The court was hearing a writ petition by NALSAR alumni Mihira Sood and Abhishek Tiwari challenging BCI chairman Manan Kumar Mishra's August 13 directions to bar NALSAR's 2026 graduating batch from enrolment and order an inquiry over a campaign against the CJI.
- the BCI later withdrew those directions after public backlash.
- The bench declared the August 13 communications and any subsequent modified versions were "without any authority of law," made absolute its earlier order protecting NALSAR students and faculty from punitive action by the BCI or state bar councils, and disposed of the petition.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.1; Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.7; News Trail Mangaluru p.1; The Hindu Mangaluru p.3; The Hindu Mangaluru p.10_

#### 'Let's both show our degrees': Abhijeet Dipke challenges PM

**In short:** Cockroach Janta Party founder Abhijeet Dipke has challenged Prime Minister Narendra Modi to make their educational degrees public, reigniting controversy over Dipke's educational credentials at Boston University. In a post on X, Dipke said he was willing to show his degree from the US university if Prime Minister Modi agreed to disclose his qualifications.

Cockroach Janta Party founder Abhijeet Dipke has challenged Prime Minister Narendra Modi to make their educational degrees public, reigniting controversy over Dipke's educational credentials at Boston University. In a post on X, Dipke said he was willing to show his degree from the US university if Prime Minister Modi agreed to disclose his qualifications. The issue resurfaced after a recent podcast hosted by Prakhar Gupta, in conversation with Sonam Wangchuk, included a clip discussing a friend who studied at Boston University and had participated in protests associated with Dipke's CJP.

#### Hyderabad: Detonators seized, case booked

**In short:** As many as 160 detonators were found abandoned in a private real estate venture in the Secunderabad Cantonment on September 2, under Bolarum police limits, during intensified checks after assault rifles, pistols, magazines and ammunition were stolen from an Army facility on August 31. A senior police official said the recovery is not linked to the Army theft and that the detonators are suspected to have been used by land developers more than a year ago to explode boulders.

#### Protester at Jarange's Maratha quota stir dies

**In short:** Dashrath Raikar, a resident of Sangam about 100 km from Jalna, died on Thursday of a suspected heart attack after travelling to Antarwali Sarati village to support activist Manoj Jarange's six-day hunger strike for Maratha reservation. Fellow protesters found him unresponsive on Thursday morning after he slept in a parking area near the protest site Wednesday night; doctors at a government hospital in Ambad declared him dead, and his body was sent for post-mortem to confirm the cause.

#### Andhra Pradesh ACB arrests TTD top officer for graft

**In short:** Andhra Pradesh Anti-Corruption Bureau officials on Thursday arrested Tirumala Tirupati Devasthanams Deputy Executive Officer B Lokanatham on corruption charges after raids at his residence and office that began on Wednesday morning. ACB teams seized documents pertaining to properties worth Rs 5 crore, along with 2 kg of gold, 6.5 kg of silver and Rs 12 lakh in cash, following a tip-off that he possessed assets disproportionate to his known sources of income.

Andhra Pradesh Anti-Corruption Bureau officials on Thursday arrested Tirumala Tirupati Devasthanams Deputy Executive Officer B Lokanatham on corruption charges after raids at his residence and office that began on Wednesday morning. ACB teams seized documents pertaining to properties worth Rs 5 crore, along with 2 kg of gold, 6.5 kg of silver and Rs 12 lakh in cash, following a tip-off that he possessed assets disproportionate to his known sources of income. The case was registered based on information that emerged during the interrogation of Chinna Appanna, personal aide to former TTD chairman Y V Subba Reddy in the adulterated-ghee case.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Andhra Pradesh ACB arrests TTD top officer for graft
- **Told:** Andhra Pradesh Anti-Corruption Bureau officials on Thursday arrested Tirumala Tirupati Devasthanams Deputy Executive Officer B Lokanatham on corruption charges after raids at his residence and office that began on Wednesday morning.

#### Two rescued from bonded labour

**In short:** Two persons were rescued from alleged bonded labour in a raid at a cowshed in Wadvani tehsil, Beed district, after legal aid volunteers flagged the matter to Collector Vivek Johnson and DLSA chairman Dinesh P Surana ordered an immediate rescue. The two were allegedly kept captive, assaulted and forced to work up to 15 hours a day for two months; five other workers remain missing after the owner allegedly moved them under cover of darkness, and Tehsildar Vaibhav Mahendrakar was bitten by a dog during the September 1 raid amid power cuts and no mobile connectivity.

#### Ram Temple 'corporatisation' has started, says Raut

**In short:** Shiv Sena (UBT) leader Sanjay Raut said on Thursday that appointing retired Air Marshal Jeetendra Mishra as the first CEO of the Shri Ram Janmabhoomi Teerth Kshetra Trust, alongside a major overhaul after donation-theft allegations, means the Ayodhya temple's "corporatisation" has begun. Raut also questioned Prime Minister Narendra Modi's praise of 7.8 per cent GDP growth, citing former finance secretary Subhash Garg's claim that growth would have been about 2.6 per cent in current prices without last year's GDP revision down from roughly Rs 86 lakh crore to Rs 80 lakh crore, and asked why the government still gives free food grains to 85 crore citizens and Rs 1500 under Ladki Bahin.

#### Social media influencer arrested for 'objectionable' remarks against Maha Min

**In short:** Pune police arrested 45-year-old social media influencer Santosh Pandit from his residence on Thursday for allegedly posting obscene, sexually suggestive and defamatory YouTube content against Maharashtra Higher and Technical Education Minister Chandrakant Patil and a public representative, with objectionable remarks about Chief Minister Devendra Fadnavis in another video. Kothrud police registered the case at 1:30 am under BNS sections 75(1)(iv), 75(3), 356(2) and 352 and Section 67 of the IT Act; DCP Sagar Kawade confirmed the arrest, and this is Pandit's second Pune case over remarks against Patil after an earlier complaint at Deccan police station.

#### Edu is a right, marginalised students must not face neglect: Rahul Gandhi

**In short:** Congress MP Rahul Gandhi said during his Wednesday visit to Nashik that education is not a privilege but the right of every student in India, and students from marginalised communities should not face neglect from the system. He met a delegation of tribal students, including some on hunger strike over issues including the condition of ashram schools meant for tribals, listened to their problems, and asserted that every student deserves a respectful, safe and conducive study environment with full system support.

#### Goa to provide 3 free LPG cylinders to citizens annually

**In short:** Goa Chief Minister Pramod Sawant announced on Thursday that the state will provide three LPG cylinders free of cost annually by crediting Rs 3,000 every year into beneficiaries' bank accounts from September 9, fulfilling a 2022 assembly election promise. Forms will be available at panchayats and with MLAs from September 5; beneficiaries must submit copies of bank passbook, Aadhaar card and voter identity card to receive the assistance toward three cylinders.

#### Activist seeking ban on music ahead of Ganesh festival assaulted in Pune

**In short:** City-based activist Vidyanand Bapat, who has vocally opposed high-decibel DJ systems and loudspeakers before Ganeshotsav, was slapped by Santosh Chavan while speaking to electronic media on Tilak Road around 11 am Thursday; the assault was captured on camera and police escorted Bapat away. Chief Minister Devendra Fadnavis said the attacker was arrested and assaulting anyone for voicing views was wrong, while Pune Police Commissioner Amitesh Kumar pledged strict action; Bapat had shot to prominence last month when Ganesh mandal representatives became aggressive after he sought a ban on amplified DJ music at a Pune Municipal Corporation meeting ahead of the September 14 festival.

#### Telangana CM sacks Forest Minister Konda Surekha

**In short:** Telangana Chief Minister A Revanth Reddy decided on Thursday to drop Forest and Endowments Minister Konda Surekha from the Council of Ministers with immediate effect and recommended the move to Governor Shiv Pratap Shukla, amid a row over critical remarks by her daughter against the chief minister and other party leaders. He also rescinded G Chinna Reddy's appointment as Vice Chairman of the State Planning Board and appointed Women's Commission Chairperson Gadwal Vijayalakshmi as the new VC, following confabulations with AICC leaders in Delhi after the state Congress disciplinary committee recommended tough action against Surekha and Chinna Reddy.

#### Oppn slams BJP as Nabin welcomes Russian envoy with 'Rasputin' playing in background

**In short:** When BJP chief Nitin Nabin met Russian envoy Denis Alipov at party headquarters on Tuesday, he shared a video of the meeting with Boney M’s 1978 hit “Ra Ra Rasputin” playing in the background, drawing criticism from Congress and Trinamool leaders including Abhishek Manu Singhvi, Supriya Shrinate, Mahua Moitra, and Saket Gokhale, who called it comical, tasteless, and insulting to Russia because the song is widely used in memes about Vladimir Putin. Nabin said the meeting was warm and productive, covering India-Russia ties and the BJP’s history and organisation, and the BJP is yet to comment on the opposition criticism.

#### Aid to schools in WB to be cut if Vande Mataram is not sung in

**In short:** West Bengal Chief Minister Suvendu Adhikari warned on Thursday that financial aid and other government support to schools would be stopped if the mandatory singing of the full version of Vande Mataram during school prayers was not strictly followed. Addressing an RSS-affiliated organisation in Kolkata, he said the BJP dispensation was undertaking reforms to restore Indian culture and nationalist values in school education, and claimed scholarships from classes 5–8 under the previous TMC government were made available only to Muslim students.

West Bengal Chief Minister Suvendu Adhikari warned on Thursday that financial aid and other government support to schools would be stopped if the mandatory singing of the full version of Vande Mataram during school prayers was not strictly followed. Addressing an RSS-affiliated organisation in Kolkata, he said the BJP dispensation was undertaking reforms to restore Indian culture and nationalist values in school education, and claimed scholarships from classes 5–8 under the previous TMC government were made available only to Muslim students. He also said he had written to Defence Minister Rajnath Singh seeking permission to make NCC mandatory in schools and colleges.

**What this is**

#### Satheesan's FB post against Islamic scholar missing

**In short:** A post by Kerala Chief Minister V D Satheesan criticising senior Islamic scholar Kanthapuram A P Aboobacker Musliyar over alleged anti-women remarks has gone missing from his Facebook page, though it remains on Instagram, the CMO said on Thursday attributing the disappearance to a technical error from linking between the platforms. The post contained a portion of video from his Wednesday press conference; the CMO rejected reports that it was removed after protests by Sunni Muslim leaders who met Satheesan at the CMO.

#### ED raids in Keralam in drug smuggling case

**In short:** The Enforcement Directorate conducted raids at multiple locations across Kerala on Thursday in a money-laundering probe linked to two drug-smuggling cases, including the June seizure of about 18 kg of hybrid ganja worth around Rs 18 crore from Perumbavoor and the October seizure of MDMA smuggled through Karipur airport in Malappuram. Searches were conducted at residences of accused persons including Abu Tahir, Harikrishnan, Jagath, and Jaisal in Kozhikode and Palakkad districts; a preliminary probe found they allegedly laundered large sums from trafficking hybrid ganja mainly smuggled from Thailand.

#### Row over what was served for Sabarimala Onam feast

**In short:** Differences have emerged between Travancore Devaswom Board president K Jayakumar and Kerala Devaswom Minister K Muraleedharan over Onam sadya served to devotees at Sabarimala, with Jayakumar initially denying reports that devotees got pulao while officials received sadya, and Muraleedharan claiming devotees were given pulao on Thiruvonam day. Jayakumar later acknowledged pulao was served at the Annadanam hall while sadya was organised in the staff mess, but said about 3,000 devotees who came for pulao were also served sadya.

#### SC dismisses PIL challenging curbs on entry of journalists to schools

**In short:** The Supreme Court has dismissed a PIL by Priya Mishra challenging restrictions imposed by Rajasthan and Uttar Pradesh education departments on entry of journalists, YouTubers, social-media personalities and civil-society members to government schools, including curbs on photography, videography, interviews, audio recording and live-streaming. A bench of Justices P S Narasimha and Alok Aradhe said in its September 1 order that it was not inclined to entertain the Article 32 petition, including one challenging an August 16 Rajasthan circular requiring prior principal permission for outsiders.

#### WIDESPREAD DAMAGE AFTER RAINS LASH UP'S THREE DISTRICTS; FIVE KILLED

**In short:** Five people, including three members of a family, were killed in separate rain-related incidents in Uttar Pradesh's Chitrakoot, Amethi and Barabanki districts, officials said on Thursday. A mud house collapse in Chitrakoot's Barachhi village killed Kallu Yadav (32) and two children; in Amethi, 66-year-old Shyamlal Chauhan died when a mud wall collapsed; and in Barabanki, 62-year-old Shalik Ram Kashyap died after a rain-weakened mud wall fell on him and his son-in-law, who survived.

#### Tata Power commissions transmission corridor

**In short:** Tata Power on Thursday said it has commissioned the 400 kV Jalpura-Khurja transmission corridor in Uttar Pradesh, comprising a double-circuit transmission line from Khurja to Jalpura spanning 162 circuit kilometres and a gas-insulated switchgear substation at Jalpura with 1,000 MVA transformation capacity. Khurja is an important power node in Western Uttar Pradesh, playing a strategic role in enabling efficient transmission of power towards NCR and Noida.

#### RPG Active Pharma acquires API biz

**In short:** RPG Life Sciences Ltd's wholly-owned subsidiary, RPG Active Pharma Ltd, has entered into a business transfer agreement to acquire the active pharmaceutical ingredients (API) and intermediates business of Raghava Life Sciences for an amount up to Rs 135 crore. The transaction will be executed on a going-concern basis through a slump sale, subject to customary closing conditions and regulatory approvals, and includes a portfolio of 22 commercialised APIs and seven development-stage assets across therapeutic segments such as diabetes, cardiovascular and CNS.

#### India-NZ trade likely by next month

**In short:** The India-New Zealand free trade agreement is likely to come into force next month on a mutually agreed date, an official said on Thursday, after the two countries signed the FTA on April 27 to boost two-way commerce in goods and services and promote investments. The pact provides duty-free access for 100 per cent of India's exports to New Zealand, which had maintained peak tariffs of up to 10 per cent on key Indian exports including ceramics, carpets, automobiles and auto components, and New Zealand has committed to invest USD 20 billion over 15 years.

#### Inox Wind bags Rs 755-cr turnkey project

**In short:** Inox Wind on Thursday said it has secured a 100 MW wind energy turnkey order worth Rs 755 crore from Indian Oil Corporation, to be executed on a turnkey basis including post-commissioning operations and maintenance services. Under the order, Inox Wind will handle end-to-end project execution, including supply of wind turbine generators, engineering, procurement and construction, project execution, and post-commissioning O&M services.

#### Prasol Chemicals fixes IPO price band

**In short:** Specialty chemicals manufacturer Prasol Chemicals Ltd is gearing up to launch its Rs 500-crore Initial Public Offering on September 8, with the price band fixed at Rs 643-676 per equity share; the IPO will close on September 10 and bidding for anchor investors will take place on September 7. The Rs 500-crore IPO comprises a fresh issue of equity shares worth up to Rs 80 crore and an Offer For Sale of shares worth up to Rs 420 crore by selling shareholders, with proceeds from the fresh issue to be used for payment of debt, working capital requirements and general corporate purposes.

#### Adani Group bets big in non-BJP states

**In short:** The Adani Group is expanding its presence across Congress-led and other non-Bharatiya Janata Party-ruled states, pursuing or executing projects spanning roads, ports, power, renewable energy, cement and data centres totalling about Rs 5 lakh crore as state governments seek private capital for economic development, infrastructure and job creation. In Karnataka, Adani Enterprises emerged as the lowest bidder for both packages of the proposed 16.75-kilometre north-south tunnel road connecting Hebbal and Central Silk Board in Bengaluru at Rs 22,267 crore, Rs 3,500 crore lower than the second-lowest bidder; the group also has the 1,200-megawatt Udupi power plant and Mangaluru International Airport, with further investments across Kerala, Telangana, Tamil Nadu, Bihar and Andhra Pradesh.

#### 'Auto companies must prepare for end of EV subsidies'

**In short:** Government subsidies for electric vehicles will end in the coming years and companies need to be prepared for the challenge, Kamran Rizvi, Secretary in the Heavy Industries Ministry, said on Thursday while addressing the annual convention of the Society of Indian Automobile Manufacturers. He urged automobile and component makers to spend more on R&D, collaborate to set up charging infrastructure, and noted that electric three-wheelers account for 50 per cent of segment sales—well above the 10 per cent target for 2026—while two-wheelers are at 7 per cent electric penetration and cars at around 4-5 per cent, with demand for electric buses outstripping supply.

#### Alliance Insurance settles over Rs 75 cr claims

**In short:** Alliance Insurance Brokers settled more than Rs 75 crore in insurance claims across jewellery and specie-related risks during the financial year ended March 2026, with employee dishonesty, theft and transit losses emerging as key areas of exposure. The broker settled Rs 32.26 crore in jewellery and specie insurance claims across 162 cases during FY2025-26 involving organised jewellery chains, wholesalers, exporters, manufacturers, retailers, bullion dealers, gold-for-loan businesses and individual customers, with employee or karigar dishonesty accounting for the largest share of claims by number and value.

The broker settled Rs 32.26 crore in jewellery and specie insurance claims across 162 cases during FY2025-26 involving organised jewellery chains, wholesalers, exporters, manufacturers, retailers, bullion dealers, gold-for-loan businesses and individual customers, with employee or karigar dishonesty accounting for the largest share of claims by number and value.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.11_

#### US trade pact to be ready when preferential rates are offered

**In short:** Commerce and Industry Minister Piyush Goyal on Thursday said the details of the Bilateral Trade Agreement with the US will be announced once Washington offers preferential terms compared to the country's competitors. He said India's nine free trade agreements spanning economies representing about USD 60 trillion of GDP would provide preferential access to nearly two-thirds of global trade, and that other FTAs with Canada, Mexico, Chile, Mercosur, SACU, GCC and Israel, together with efforts to review ASEAN, Korea and Japan deals, would give India access to 75 per cent of global trade at a rate lower than its competitors.

#### Lumino shares debut at 34% premium

**In short:** Shares of Lumino Industries made a strong debut on bourses on Thursday, listing with a premium of more than 34 per cent against the issue price of Rs 82; the stock opened at Rs 110 on the NSE, a 34.15 per cent premium, and later jumped 44.15 per cent to Rs 118.20, while on the BSE it listed at Rs 109 and surged 45.11 per cent to Rs 118.99. The Rs 700-crore IPO in a price band of Rs 78-82 was subscribed 118.12 times on the final day of bidding on Monday, comprising a fresh issue worth up to Rs 500 crore and an Offer-for-Sale of up to Rs 200 crore by promoters Devendra Goel and Jay Goel.

#### Supreme Court dismisses SEBI's plea against NSE

**In short:** The Supreme Court on Thursday disposed of pleas by the Securities and Exchange Board of India against the National Stock Exchange in the co-location and dark fibre cases after the market regulator approved around ₹1,500-crore settlement with the exchange. The co-location and dark fibre cases relate to allegations that certain stockbrokers received unfair, preferential speed advantages to access market data and trade before other investors; in July the NSE paid ₹714.74 crore to SEBI after receiving in-principle approval to settle the long-pending cases for ₹1,491.21 crore, and the latest payment completes the settlement amount.

#### Sebi mulls net settlement of cash market obligations for MFs

**In short:** Markets regulator Sebi on Thursday proposed permitting net settlement of funds for transactions undertaken by mutual fund schemes in the cash market, while continuing settlement of securities on a gross basis, aiming to reduce temporary liquidity requirements. The proposal is intended to facilitate ease of doing business, improve settlement efficiency and reduce temporary liquidity requirements for mutual fund schemes, while ensuring that existing safeguards relating to delivery-based settlement, scheme-wise accounting, valuation and investor protection remain unaffected, and comes after representations from market participants highlighting liquidity and operational inefficiencies during index rebalancing and large investor subscriptions or redemptions.

#### India gets first Asian Games safeguarding officer in Neetal

**In short:** Following an Olympic Council of Asia (OCA) mandate, Neetal Narang will be India's first Safeguarding Officer at the Asian Games, serving as athletes' contact for harassment, abuse or neglect at the September 19–October 4 Aichi-Nagoya Games. President of the Softball Association of India and an internationally certified safeguarding officer, Narang will work with the OCA safeguarding officer to handle confidential complaints.

Following an Olympic Council of Asia (OCA) mandate, Neetal Narang will be India's first Safeguarding Officer at the Asian Games, serving as athletes' contact for harassment, abuse or neglect at the September 19–October 4 Aichi-Nagoya Games. President of the Softball Association of India and an internationally certified safeguarding officer, Narang will work with the OCA safeguarding officer to handle confidential complaints. India will field 503 athletes (269 men, 234 women) in 36 disciplines, aiming to surpass its Hangzhou best of 106 medals (28 gold, 38 silver, 40 bronze); the OCA made the role mandatory in 2022 and Om Prakash Karhana held it at last year's Youth Asian Games.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** India gets first Asian Games safeguarding officer in Neetal

_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.12_

#### Just 56 women in support staff

**In short:** India's 246-member Asian Games support staff includes only 56 women, about 22% of the total, prompting the Sports Ministry to direct the IOA to ensure adequate female doctors, physiotherapists and masseuses where squads lack them. Volleyball and E-Sports have no women support staff as only men compete, but the all-female three-member judo squad and golf's three women representatives also have no female support staff.

India's 246-member Asian Games support staff includes only 56 women, about 22% of the total, prompting the Sports Ministry to direct the IOA to ensure adequate female doctors, physiotherapists and masseuses where squads lack them. Volleyball and E-Sports have no women support staff as only men compete, but the all-female three-member judo squad and golf's three women representatives also have no female support staff. An IOA source said there is no mandate for a specific number of women officials and NSFs cannot be forced to include them, though federations can be urged to balance the ratio.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Just 56 women in support staff
- **Told:** India's 246-member Asian Games support staff includes only 56 women, about 22% of the total, prompting the Sports Ministry to direct the IOA to ensure adequate female doctors, physiotherapists and masseuses where squads lack them.

#### Jamil defends dropping seniors, says picked India squad purely on merit

**In short:** Indian football head coach Khalid Jamil defended omitting established players from the 26-man squad for friendlies against Brazil, Uruguay and Panama, saying selections were merit-based after giving everyone an equal and adequate chance. Notable exclusions include former captain Sandesh Jhingan, Rahul Bheke, Lallianzuala Chhangte, Mohun Bagan goalkeeper Vishal Kaith, Durand Cup golden glove winner Liston Colaco and East Bengal midfielder Jeakson Singh.

#### India, Belgium ink eight defence deals, boost military ties

**In short:** India and Belgium on Thursday signed eight contracts between arms companies and agreed to deploy senior defence officials in their respective embassies to promote industry cooperation, alongside a Letter of Intent for regular exchanges, staff talks and research cooperation. A Belgian frigate, Leopold, will visit India between November 5 and 8, Belgium conveyed it will not supply critical military hardware to Pakistan, and agreements cover ammunition, rockets, autonomous maritime systems, drones, counter-drones and armoured-vehicle systems.

India and Belgium on Thursday signed eight contracts between arms companies and agreed to deploy senior defence officials in their respective embassies to promote industry cooperation, alongside a Letter of Intent for regular exchanges, staff talks and research cooperation. A Belgian frigate, Leopold, will visit India between November 5 and 8, Belgium conveyed it will not supply critical military hardware to Pakistan, and agreements cover ammunition, rockets, autonomous maritime systems, drones, counter-drones and armoured-vehicle systems. Fifteen Belgian companies accompanied Prime Minister Narendra Modi and Belgian counterpart Bart De Wever during the visit.

**What this is**

#### Kharge questions delay in FIR over 'purification' at Haldwani

**In short:** Congress president Mallikarjun Kharge on Thursday questioned the delay in filing an FIR for carrying out 'shudhikaran' at the site of his rally in Haldwani and asked Rajya Sabha Leader of the House JP Nadda to honour the promise he made in the Rajya Sabha to investigate the "act of untouchability". Kharge urged Nadda to direct Uttarakhand Chief Minister Pushkar Dhami to act on the incident that took place more than three weeks ago, and faulted Prime Minister Narendra Modi and Home Minister Amit Shah for their "silence" on the issue.

#### Centre unveils defence diplomacy blueprint

**In short:** Defence Minister Rajnath Singh on Thursday released 'Raksha' — a strategic blueprint on defence diplomacy outlining India's roadmap and vision for global defence partnerships over the next decade. The Ministry of Defence said the document provides a comprehensive framework aligning national security objectives with proactive global cooperation, with key pillars including strategic partnerships, capacity building, indigenous exports and maritime security.

Defence Minister Rajnath Singh on Thursday released 'Raksha' — a strategic blueprint on defence diplomacy outlining India's roadmap and vision for global defence partnerships over the next decade. The Ministry of Defence said the document provides a comprehensive framework aligning national security objectives with proactive global cooperation, with key pillars including strategic partnerships, capacity building, indigenous exports and maritime security. Under maritime security, the vision seeks to strengthen India's role as a net-security provider and first responder in the Indian Ocean Region.

#### 31-member JPC set up to examine foreign funding bill

**In short:** A 31-member multi-party Joint Parliamentary Committee headed by BJP's Sanjay Jaiswal was constituted on Thursday to scrutinise the Foreign Contribution Regulation (Amendment) Bill, 2026, which proposes a designated authority to manage and dispose of assets if an organisation loses its FCRA licence. The panel has 21 MPs from the Lok Sabha and 10 from the Rajya Sabha, with 19 NDA members and the rest from the Opposition, and has been asked to submit its report by the last day of the first week of the winter session.

A 31-member multi-party Joint Parliamentary Committee headed by BJP's Sanjay Jaiswal was constituted on Thursday to scrutinise the Foreign Contribution Regulation (Amendment) Bill, 2026, which proposes a designated authority to manage and dispose of assets if an organisation loses its FCRA licence. The panel has 21 MPs from the Lok Sabha and 10 from the Rajya Sabha, with 19 NDA members and the rest from the Opposition, and has been asked to submit its report by the last day of the first week of the winter session. Lok Sabha Speaker Om Birla constituted the panel 22 days after Parliament adopted a motion to send the bill to a joint committee; the bill had divided the ruling NDA as well as the Opposition.

#### Centre tells SC it is examining CBSE three-language policy

**In short:** The Centre on Thursday informed the Supreme Court that it is examining suggestions for implementing the Central Board of Secondary Education's three-language policy, which requires Class 9 students to study three languages including two Indian languages. Additional Solicitor General Aishwarya Bhati submitted that the CBSE is finalising various modalities and sought time; the bench accepted the request and deferred the matter by a week.

The Centre on Thursday informed the Supreme Court that it is examining suggestions for implementing the Central Board of Secondary Education's three-language policy, which requires Class 9 students to study three languages including two Indian languages. Additional Solicitor General Aishwarya Bhati submitted that the CBSE is finalising various modalities and sought time; the bench accepted the request and deferred the matter by a week. The court had earlier on August 20 directed the CBSE to consider granting a one-time reprieve to Class 6 students from the mandatory third-language requirement and questioned classifying English as a non-indigenous language under the revised policy.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Centre tells SC it is examining CBSE three-language policy

#### Fort St George preserves 400 yrs of history

**In short:** Chief Minister C Joseph Vijay announced on Thursday that the Tamil Nadu government's Secretariat would be shifted out of Fort St George, the historic building that has served as the administrative centre since 1652 after land was bought in 1640. Spread over 107 acres, the heritage complex houses the Chief Minister's office, key departments including Home and Finance, the Tamil Nadu Legislative Assembly and a museum with over 3,661 antiquities; its six-metre-high walls withstood assaults including from Hyder Ali and Tipu Sultan in the 18th century.

Chief Minister C Joseph Vijay announced on Thursday that the Tamil Nadu government's Secretariat would be shifted out of Fort St George, the historic building that has served as the administrative centre since 1652 after land was bought in 1640. Spread over 107 acres, the heritage complex houses the Chief Minister's office, key departments including Home and Finance, the Tamil Nadu Legislative Assembly and a museum with over 3,661 antiquities; its six-metre-high walls withstood assaults including from Hyder Ali and Tipu Sultan in the 18th century. Historians like Venkatesh Ramakrishnan want the government and military to move out over a 20-year period, turning the entire complex into an open-access Heritage Village.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Fort St George preserves 400 yrs of history
- **Told:** Chief Minister C Joseph Vijay announced on Thursday that the Tamil Nadu government's Secretariat would be shifted out of Fort St George, the historic building that has served as the administrative centre since 1652 after land was bought in 1640.
- **Purpose:** Historians like Venkatesh Ramakrishnan want the government and military to move out over a 20-year period, turning the entire complex into an open-access Heritage Village.

#### 'Cracked his skull': Jantar Mantar claim sparks row

**In short:** A right-wing activist claiming credit in a video interview for the attack on a protester's father at Jantar Mantar during the students' agitation has triggered an uproar, with Rahul Gandhi on Thursday pledging support to the victim's daughter Nishu Azad and the Cockroach Janta Party announcing they will approach the police. Swatantra Bhardwaj was heard saying he "cracked the skull" of Nishu Azad's father Sanjay on June 23, that the injury needed 60 stitches and attracted Section 307, and that he was released after Delhi BJP leader Kapil Mishra's call.

#### Nagaland urges Centre to roll back regulation for foreigners

**In short:** The Nagaland Assembly on Thursday unanimously passed a resolution urging the Centre to withdraw its Protected Area Permit system, which regulates the travel of foreign visitors to the frontier state. The resolution, moved by Deputy Chief Minister and BJP legislator Yanthungo Patton, stated that the PAP regime had hampered the growth of international tourism, investment, business engagement, cultural exchange and international partnerships.

The Nagaland Assembly on Thursday unanimously passed a resolution urging the Centre to withdraw its Protected Area Permit system, which regulates the travel of foreign visitors to the frontier state. The resolution, moved by Deputy Chief Minister and BJP legislator Yanthungo Patton, stated that the PAP regime had hampered the growth of international tourism, investment, business engagement, cultural exchange and international partnerships. The House assured that appropriate systems for registration, regulation and monitoring of foreign nationals could be put in place through modern technology, noting that the Ministry of Home Affairs on December 17, 2024 reinforced the regime in Nagaland, Manipur and Mizoram after earlier relaxations from January 1, 2011.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Nagaland urges Centre to roll back regulation for foreigners
- **Told:** The Nagaland Assembly on Thursday unanimously passed a resolution urging the Centre to withdraw its Protected Area Permit system, which regulates the travel of foreign visitors to the frontier state.

**Important points**
- The House assured that appropriate systems for registration, regulation and monitoring of foreign nationals could be put in place through modern technology, noting that the Ministry of Home Affairs on December 17, 2024 reinforced the regime in Nagaland.
- Manipur and Mizoram after earlier relaxations from January 1, 2011.

#### Orissa's starvation deaths shock SC

**In short:** The Supreme Court expressed shock over how the Orissa government handled starvation deaths and asked why below-poverty-line families received only 25 kg of food grains per month when government regulation provided for 73 kg. Attorney General Soli Sorabjee told a division bench that the Centre had issued orders for speedy supply of food grains from Food Corporation of India godowns to the public distribution system by the states.

#### Jagjivan calls for three-pronged strategy on food

**In short:** Union Agriculture Minister Jagjivan Ram, inaugurating a three-day Association of Food Scientists and Technologists symposium in Bangalore on "Strategies for storage, handling and processing of foodgrains in the present-day context," called on scientists and food technologists to evolve a three-pronged grain reserve strategy. It should combine buffer stocks, reserves for the regular needs of the PDS, and surplus stocks for export.

#### Why not have cameras, monitoring rooms at toll plazas, SC asks NHAI

**In short:** The Supreme Court on Thursday asked the National Highways Authority of India (NHAI) why it cannot deploy camera surveillance systems and monitoring rooms at toll plazas to tackle rampant encroachment and stationary vehicles clogging national highways. A bench of Justices Vikram Nath and Sandeep Mehta was hearing a suo motu case arising from a November 2, 2025 accident in Rajasthan’s Phalodi area that claimed 15 lives, and noted that encroachment and illegally parked vehicles were major contributors to road accidents.

The Supreme Court on Thursday asked the National Highways Authority of India (NHAI) why it cannot deploy camera surveillance systems and monitoring rooms at toll plazas to tackle rampant encroachment and stationary vehicles clogging national highways. A bench of Justices Vikram Nath and Sandeep Mehta was hearing a suo motu case arising from a November 2, 2025 accident in Rajasthan’s Phalodi area that claimed 15 lives, and noted that encroachment and illegally parked vehicles were major contributors to road accidents. Unconvinced by NHAI’s submission that circulars had already been issued for inspection and patrolling, the court suggested a more proactive approach and listed the matter for October 6.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Why not have cameras, monitoring rooms at toll plazas, SC asks NHAI
- **Told:** The Supreme Court on Thursday asked the National Highways Authority of India (NHAI) why it cannot deploy camera surveillance systems and monitoring rooms at toll plazas to tackle rampant encroachment and stationary vehicles clogging national highways.
- **Purpose:** Unconvinced by NHAI’s submission that circulars had already been issued for inspection and patrolling, the court suggested a more proactive approach and listed the matter for October 6.

**Important points**

_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.10; The Hindu Mangaluru p.12_

#### Gujarat man jailed in US for smuggling fentanyl chemicals

**In short:** A US court has sentenced Surat businessman Bhavesh Lathiya to 54 months — 4.5 years — in prison for distributing and smuggling fentanyl-precursor chemicals from India by mislabelling them as Vitamin C and using other “deceptive and fraudulent practices”. United States District Judge Pamela K Chen also ordered Lathiya, founder of Surat-based Raxuter Chemicals, to pay a forfeiture of $24,560 (a little more than Rs 23 lakh); he pleaded guilty in April and will be deported to India after completing his jail term.

A US court has sentenced Surat businessman Bhavesh Lathiya to 54 months — 4.5 years — in prison for distributing and smuggling fentanyl-precursor chemicals from India by mislabelling them as Vitamin C and using other “deceptive and fraudulent practices”. United States District Judge Pamela K Chen also ordered Lathiya, founder of Surat-based Raxuter Chemicals, to pay a forfeiture of $24,560 (a little more than Rs 23 lakh); he pleaded guilty in April and will be deported to India after completing his jail term. The US Attorney’s Office said his firm sold and smuggled more than 50 pounds of chemicals used to manufacture fentanyl, a synthetic opioid said to be 100 times more powerful than morphine.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Gujarat man jailed in US for smuggling fentanyl chemicals
- **Told:** A US court has sentenced Surat businessman Bhavesh Lathiya to 54 months — 4.5 years — in prison for distributing and smuggling fentanyl-precursor chemicals from India by mislabelling them as Vitamin C and using other “deceptive and fraudulent practices”.
- **Purpose:** The US Attorney’s Office said his firm sold and smuggled more than 50 pounds of chemicals used to manufacture fentanyl, a synthetic opioid said to be 100 times more powerful than morphine.

**Important points**
- The US Attorney’s Office said his firm sold and smuggled more than 50 pounds of chemicals used to manufacture fentanyl, a synthetic opioid said to be 100 times more powerful than morphine.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.10_

#### Wife can't be expected to behave like Vodafone pug: HC

**In short:** The Madurai Bench of the Madras High Court set aside a trial court order refusing divorce to an estranged couple, observing that a wife cannot be expected to behave like the pug in the Vodafone advertisement and that it may not always be feasible for a husband to take his wife along whenever he is transferred. Justices GR Swaminathan and MD Sumathi granted divorce to a couple living apart for 16 years, directing the husband to pay Rs 7 lakh as alimony, and held that where a marriage has broken down irretrievably, continuing it would only mean giving sanction to cruelty.

#### 160 detonators found near Secunderabad Army camp

**In short:** Three days after theft of service weapons and ammunition from the 20 Madras Regiment premises in Bollarum, Secunderabad Cantonment, 160 electric detonators were found near the Army camp on Wednesday, though police said they were unrelated to the arms theft and allegedly left behind during construction of a venture beside the golf course. Bollarum police registered a case against unidentified persons after a bomb disposal team identified the material as 33A-size commercial electric detonators packed in eight bundles in a plastic bucket near the firing range rear wall.

#### TN to facilitate DNA tests of kin of those missing in Nepal floods

**In short:** The Tamil Nadu government said it would facilitate DNA tests of willing first-degree relatives of 85 people still missing after Nepal flash floods, of 305 pilgrims from the state who went to Kailash Mansarovar; 199 have returned on their own and 21 caught in the floods were rescued. Families can provide DNA samples at designated government laboratories in Chennai, Thanjavur and Madurai after the Nepal government requested samples from relatives of the missing.

#### FLY91 inks firm order for 40 ATR aircraft

**In short:** Regional airline FLY91 signed a firm order for 40 ATR 72-600 aircraft valued at about $1 billion, the largest ATR order globally by any regional airline, and will lease another six to eight aircraft before deliveries begin. Unveiled in 2024 with 280 weekly flights to 12 destinations including Pune, Hubballi, Bengaluru, Goa, Kochi and Hyderabad, the airline marks ATR’s first big Indian order since IndiGo’s 50-aircraft deal in 2017.

#### Services sector growth rises to 4-year high

**In short:** India’s services sector growth improved in August from an over four-year low in the previous month, supported by new orders that also helped job creation, according to an S&P Global industry survey released on Thursday. India’s services PMI rose to 54.1 in August from 53.3 in July, though the August figure was the second-weakest since March 2022 and remained below its long-run average of 54.5.

India’s services sector growth improved in August from an over four-year low in the previous month, supported by new orders that also helped job creation, according to an S&P Global industry survey released on Thursday. India’s services PMI rose to 54.1 in August from 53.3 in July, though the August figure was the second-weakest since March 2022 and remained below its long-run average of 54.5. Employment growth hit a 15-month high, while prices charged rose at the fastest pace since March as firms passed on higher operating costs.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Services sector growth rises to 4-year high
- **Told:** India’s services sector growth improved in August from an over four-year low in the previous month, supported by new orders that also helped job creation, according to an S&P Global industry survey released on Thursday.
- **Purpose:** Employment growth hit a 15-month high, while prices charged rose at the fastest pace since March as firms passed on higher operating costs.

**Important points**
- Employment growth hit a 15-month high, while prices charged rose at the fastest pace since March as firms passed on higher operating costs.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.12_

#### India’s $136 billion haul aids rupee, but ups future risks

**In short:** The Indian rupee climbed to a two-month high on Thursday after a much larger-than-expected $136.4 billion raised through one-off measures improved the currency’s outlook at a time when renewed US-Iran conflict has pushed crude oil prices higher. The massive fundraising further swells the Reserve Bank of India’s already-record future dollar liabilities, potentially putting a ceiling on rupee appreciation; Nomura analysts estimate India’s balance of payments will be in a surplus of about $66 billion in the current fiscal year, against a deficit of $23.6 billion in the previous year.

The Indian rupee climbed to a two-month high on Thursday after a much larger-than-expected $136.4 billion raised through one-off measures improved the currency’s outlook at a time when renewed US-Iran conflict has pushed crude oil prices higher. #### 32% of India’s workforce are frontier professionals: Report

**In short:** About 32% of India’s workforce are frontier professionals — people redesigning work around AI agents — double the global average of 16%, the Microsoft Work Trend Index 2026 showed. The global research, conducted with 20,000 AI users across 10 markets, found 78% of India’s AI users say the technology enables work that was not possible a year ago, compared with 58% globally.

About 32% of India’s workforce are frontier professionals — people redesigning work around AI agents — double the global average of 16%, the Microsoft Work Trend Index 2026 showed. The global research, conducted with 20,000 AI users across 10 markets, found 78% of India’s AI users say the technology enables work that was not possible a year ago, compared with 58% globally. Puneet Chandok, President, Microsoft India & South Asia, said India has the world’s largest frontier workforce building up.

#### Bharat NCAP 2.0 to be rolled out from October 2027, says Gadkari

**In short:** The Centre is planning to roll out a new version of the Bharat NCAP programme from October 2027, which will assess the complete safety framework and address the severe challenge of road accidents in the country, Union Minister Nitin Gadkari said on Thursday at the 66th SIAM Annual Convention 2026. He also announced a scheme for a rare emission control device for BS IV-compliant vehicles, costing Rs 4,000-5,000, with trials completed showing emissions after retrofitment on BS 4 vehicles same as BS 6.

#### PayPal lays off 220 staffers in India

**In short:** Digital payments platform PayPal has laid off 220 employees, representing 4% of its employees in India, as part of a previously announced multi-year transformation to simplify global operations, strengthen execution and position the company for long-term growth. The company said it is committed to supporting affected employees through the transition.

Digital payments platform PayPal has laid off 220 employees, representing 4% of its employees in India, as part of a previously announced multi-year transformation to simplify global operations, strengthen execution and position the company for long-term growth. The company said it is committed to supporting affected employees through the transition. Uber too has cut more than 3,000 jobs worldwide, which would impact Indian employees; CEO Dara Khosrowshahi said the company accumulated too many layers and small teams that slowed decision-making.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** PayPal lays off 220 staffers in India
- **Told:** Digital payments platform PayPal has laid off 220 employees, representing 4% of its employees in India, as part of a previously announced multi-year transformation to simplify global operations, strengthen execution and position the company for long-term growth.

#### SIT to probe Messi case, says Keralam Home Minister

**In short:** Keralam Home Minister Ramesh Chennithala announced formation of a Special Investigation Team to probe alleged financial misappropriation and tax evasion involving a failed bid to bring football legend Lionel Messi to the State, saying the team will conduct a detailed probe and submit a comprehensive report immediately. He said the SIT was constituted based on a note from the Chief Minister after he sought a legal opinion from the Director-General of Prosecution and found several discrepancies in the incident.

#### Ex-TDB chief P.S. Prasanth granted bail on medical terms

**In short:** The Kollam Vigilance court in Keralam granted bail to former Travancore Devaswom Board president P.S. Prasanth, arrested in connection with the alleged loss of gold from Sabarimala temple, on medical terms after he was shifted from jail to a Thiruvananthapuram hospital last month following brain haemorrhage.

#### HC asks SIT to file final report by Sept. 25 in Sabarimala case

**In short:** The Kerala High Court directed the special investigation team probing alleged misappropriation of gold from the Sabarimala temple to submit its final report before the Vigilance Court, Kollam, on or before September 25. The "stripping salt" and "cleaning salt" involved in the two cases had been forwarded to the National Metallurgical Laboratory for examination with results expected within two weeks, and the matter was posted for hearing on October 7.

#### A.P. Cabinet approves ₹4,054-cr. rural water works

**In short:** The Andhra Pradesh Cabinet approved revival and rationalisation of 13,252 rural water supply works at an estimated cost of ₹4,053 crore, sanctioned a ₹950-crore government guarantee for an APTRANSCO loan from Power Finance Corporation, and approved ₹94 crore in price deficiency support for tobacco farmers. It also approved 78 flood-damage restoration works in Krishna, NTR, Guntur and Bapatla districts at a total cost of ₹624.88 lakh and several industrial land proposals.

#### TTD ramps up medical facilities for nine-day Brahmotsavams

**In short:** The Tirumala Tirupati Devasthanams is mounting a major medical preparedness drive for the nine-day Srivari Salakatla Brahmotsavams from September 15 to 23, deploying 75 additional doctors, 60 paramedics and 16 ambulances over and above the existing strength of 11 doctors and 139 paramedical staff. Six dispensaries, 10 first-aid centres and six new medical camps will be positioned at key points across Tirumala, with Ashwini Hospital and Apollo Cardiac Centre operating round the clock during the festival.

#### Lawyers' case

**In short:** The Supreme Court on Thursday allowed young lawyers represented by advocate Prashant Bhushan, including Kunal Yadav, to approach the Delhi High Court for an independent investigation into alleged mob assault on August 21 while they were peacefully protesting at BCI premises for chairperson Manan Kumar Mishra’s resignation and regulatory reforms. Their petition said protesters were physically assaulted, dragged and forcibly thrown out inside CCTV-monitored BCI premises despite a large Delhi Police deployment including an Assistant Commissioner of Police who did not intervene.

The Supreme Court on Thursday allowed young lawyers represented by advocate Prashant Bhushan, including Kunal Yadav, to approach the Delhi High Court for an independent investigation into alleged mob assault on August 21 while they were peacefully protesting at BCI premises for chairperson Manan Kumar Mishra’s resignation and regulatory reforms. #### ED launches multi-State crackdown on narcotics networks across South

**In short:** The Enforcement Directorate on Thursday searched 30 locations across Tamil Nadu, Keralam, Karnataka and Telangana in eight narcotics-related cases under the Prevention of Money Laundering Act as part of the Nasha Mukt Bharat Abhiyan, probing money-laundering angles in NCB seizures including methamphetamine intercepts involving Sri Lankan nationals, Chennai hotel and Ambattur hauls, a Karnataka hashish and ganja case, Telangana properties linked to Kalapathi Nithu Bai, and Keralam cases involving hybrid ganja and MDMA from Muscat.

#### SIR may be legal but is not fair: former Election Commissioner

**In short:** Former Election Commissioner Ashok Lavasa told the Professor Jagdeep Singh Chhokar Memorial Lecture organised by the Association for Democratic Reforms that statutory institutions are behaving as if “might is right,” citing the special intensive revision of electoral rolls as an unfair procedure validated by the judiciary despite deletion of 13 crore names. He said authorities may claim legality but fairness matters too, warned mass anxiety could disenchant voters when 33% already do not vote, and questioned whether parties seriously opposed measures such as electoral bonds.

#### Govt. to spend ₹24,000 crore to modernise police force

**In short:** The Union government told the Supreme Court on Thursday it has begun implementing a ₹24,000-crore umbrella Police Modernisation Mission over five years to strengthen State police and Central Armed Police Forces through greater technology use. Additional Solicitor-General Rajkumar Bhaskar Thakare made the submission in a suo motu proceeding initiated in 2025 after media reports on non-functional CCTV cameras at Udaipur police stations, as the court reviewed compliance with its 2021 judgment mandating CCTV at key station locations including lock-ups and inspectors’ rooms.

#### Why perks for officials who quit to avoid inquiry: plea

**In short:** The Supreme Court issued notice to the Centre on a writ petition by Pratik Vora seeking a direction that constitutional functionaries, including judges, who resign rather than face transparent misconduct inquiries should be denied retirement perks and benefits they would have received had they completed tenure. The petition gains significance after Allahabad High Court judge Yashwant Varma’s resignation amid controversy over alleged misconduct.

#### Govt. dropped use of OBC lists for Census as those featured ‘classes, not castes’, say officials

**In short:** Officials told The Hindu the government considered using Central and State Other Backward Classes lists for the 2027 Census but finalised an open-column caste enumeration method after concluding OBC lists describe classes rather than castes, with entries such as occupational groups or “local borns” that would not qualify as castes. Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes will be enumerated using Presidential lists as before, while Opposition leaders including Rahul Gandhi and Mallikarjun Kharge have demanded explicit OBC listing rather than an open column similar to problematic caste-name proliferation in the 2011 Socio-Economic Caste Census.

#### Congress questions GDP revision, asks government to explain methodology

**In short:** The Congress on Thursday posed four questions over cumulative downward revisions of ₹43 lakh crore in estimated GDP size over four years, asking why estimates since 2022-23 were revised down, what drove the new methodology, who was consulted, and why the deflator appeared to understate inflation. Jairam Ramesh cited former Finance Secretary Subhash Chandra Garg’s calculation that nominal growth in the latest quarter would have been closer to 2.6% instead of 10.3% had earlier bases not been revised, and pointed to concerns raised by Arvind Subramanian and the IMF.

Venugopal met at Mallikarjun Kharge’s residence to discuss the leadership tussle in the Punjab unit ahead of Gandhi’s proposed yatra and elections early next year, though no official details were released. #### IAF pilot who shot down Pak. plane joins regional airline

**In short:** Former IAF Group Captain and Vir Chakra awardee Abhinandan Varthaman has joined Goa-based regional airline FLY91 as a pilot, sources said. Varthaman, who downed an enemy jet in 2019 aerial combat with Pakistan and was held captive for three days, received the Vir Chakra in 2021 for “exceptional sense of duty”; FLY91, which began flying in March 2024 with six ATR 72-600 aircraft from Goa and Hyderabad bases, said he joined in August.

#### Report warns against attempts to privatise ESIC

**In short:** ESI Ki Baat, a report on the Employees’ State Insurance Corporation prepared by Safe In India, warned the Union government against reported attempts to privatise ESIC and recommended raising the wage ceiling for coverage to ₹33,000 from ₹21,000. Releasing the report, ESIC Director General Ashok Kumar Singh said implementation of the Code on Social Security would help more workers avail ESIC benefits.

#### Manipur polls may be hit if Naga-Kuki violence continues, says Deputy CM

**In short:** Manipur Deputy Chief Minister Losii Dikho said continuing Naga-Kuki violence in hill districts could make Assembly elections next year difficult and that Naga legislators may reconsider BJP support if concerns including removal of fellow Deputy CM Nemcha Kipgen are not addressed. The NPF leader told The Hindu that at least seven people were killed in Kangpokpi over the past week, Central forces had failed to prevent attacks on Naga villages, and Naga MLAs were prepared to take “extreme steps” if the Centre did not act on their memorandum to Amit Shah seeking Kipgen’s removal.

#### States, Union Territories told to monitor glacial lakes

**In short:** Union Home Secretary Govind Mohan chaired a meeting with Himalayan States and Union Territories on Thursday to review preparedness for Glacial Lake Outburst Floods and snow avalanches, stressing proactive monitoring, risk-informed planning and stronger early warning systems in the aftermath of Nepal flash floods. He called for continuous surveillance of vulnerable glacial lakes and avalanche-prone regions and close coordination among State agencies, district administrations and scientific institutions to ensure timely action on warnings.

#### Quality elementary teachers are vital to lives of children, nation-building: top court

**In short:** The Supreme Court emphasised educating elementary school teachers, saying children’s development and the nation’s future depend on their training, ahead of Teachers’ Day on September 5. Justice P.S.

The Supreme Court emphasised educating elementary school teachers, saying children’s development and the nation’s future depend on their training, ahead of Teachers’ Day on September 5. Justice P.S. Narasimha observed that free compulsory education for ages 6–14 is now a fundamental right but teacher education lacks deserved attention, and added teacher education institutions and NCTE as sixth and seventh duty-bearers alongside government, local authority, neighbourhood school, parents and teachers under the RTE Act, 2009.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Quality elementary teachers are vital to lives of children, nation-building: top court

#### NRI deposits lift rupee to a two-month high of ₹94.6

**In short:** The Indian rupee appreciated to a two-month high of ₹94.60 against the dollar on September 3, a day after the RBI mopped up $137 billion through FCNR(B) deposits, external commercial borrowings and overseas foreign currency borrowings. The domestic currency opened at ₹94.30, down 50 paise from the previous close of ₹94.70, then gradually weakened to settle at the day’s closing level.

#### SC disposes of SEBI appeals against NSE in two cases

**In short:** The Supreme Court disposed of SEBI appeals against NSE in co-location and dark fibre cases after a nearly ₹1,500 crore settlement, taking note of about ₹1,492 crore accepted in July—₹1,224 crore for co-location and ₹268 crore for dark fibre. The cases stem from allegations that brokers received preferential access to NSE trading systems via co-location following a 2015 whistle-blower complaint, and the resolution comes as NSE prepares an IPO expected to raise around ₹30,000 crore.

#### Parliamentary panel flags 'punitive' tax regime, seeks data on new I-T Act's impact

**In short:** The Parliamentary Standing Committee on Finance, headed by BJP MP Bhartruhari Mahtab, criticised the Income Tax regime for relying excessively on “punitive action” and granting officials scope for overreach at a meeting on direct tax reforms with Revenue Department and CBDT representatives. Members flagged compliance glitches in the Income Tax Act, 2026, sought data on revenue collections, assessees and pending litigation, and expressed concern over individual taxpayers’ growing share relative to corporate collections and heavy-handed enforcement including retrospective rule changes.

#### 'Little investment in energy innovation'

**In short:** IOCL chairman A. S.

IOCL chairman A. S. Sahney lamented that barely 2% of India’s startup funding over the last 5–6 years—₹11,000 crore of about ₹5.5 lakh crore—went to energy, and excluding solar and wind it fell below ₹1,000 crore, at an MC2 Foundation event at CSIR-NCL in Pune. He said India’s energy demand will double in 15–20 years and the sector needs far more innovation and startups to match its importance as an energy nation.

- **Concept:** 'Little investment in energy innovation'

- **Purpose:** He said India’s energy demand will double in 15–20 years and the sector needs far more innovation and startups to match its importance as an energy nation.

#### No excuses left for textiles sector, says Goyal on tariffs

**In short:** Commerce Minister Piyush Goyal said India’s textile industry can no longer blame external factors, noting developed markets now impose tariffs on Indian textile imports at par with or lower than those on Bangladesh and Vietnam after LDC advantages and Vietnam’s trade deals had long put India at a disadvantage. Goyal told a workshop that rates will be better or the same as competitors in almost all developed markets and “the ball is now entirely in our court” except for performance.

#### Filatex's new unit to recycle polyester textiles

**In short:** Filatex India is investing about ₹300 crore to set up a polyester textile recycling plant in Gujarat with annual capacity of 27,000 tonne, expected to be commissioned next month. CMD Madhu Sudhan Bhageria said the plant will recycle polyester textile waste into polyester chips using in-house technology capable of recovering around 80–85% of polyester content from textile waste.

#### More than 40 crore Indians are caught in a healthcare gap: panel

**In short:** A Parliamentary Standing Committee on Health and Family Welfare report presented to Parliament last month says more than 40 crore Indians — over a quarter of the population — are too well off for government subsidies but not secure enough to absorb rising private healthcare costs, with over 60% of inpatient and 70% of outpatient care delivered privately. The panel made 368 recommendations and linked the "missing middle" vulnerability to low public health spending at 1.43% of GDP against the National Health Policy 2017 target of 2.5%, noting average private childbirth costs of ₹37,630 versus ₹2,299 in public facilities.

### International

#### Jaishankar: India ready to extend 'any help' to end Ukraine war

**In short:** External Affairs Minister Dr S Jaishankar said India has consistently maintained that this is not an era of war and that solutions will not emerge from the battlefield, after meeting Ukrainian counterpart Andrii Sybiha in Kyiv. He reiterated Prime Minister Narendra Modi's statement from Bishkek two days earlier that endless war must give way to the end of war, and expressed India's support in any manner possible to end Ukraine's conflict with Russia.

External Affairs Minister Dr S Jaishankar said India has consistently maintained that this is not an era of war and that solutions will not emerge from the battlefield, after meeting Ukrainian counterpart Andrii Sybiha in Kyiv. He reiterated Prime Minister Narendra Modi's statement from Bishkek two days earlier that endless war must give way to the end of war, and expressed India's support in any manner possible to end Ukraine's conflict with Russia. India has so far provided 19 consignments of humanitarian assistance to Ukraine, including 160 tonnes comprising generator sets, medicines, and medical equipment.

#### Viral Shaligram claim has no link to Nepal floods

**In short:** Videos circulating on social media claim that Nepal’s devastating August 26 flash floods unearthed the world’s largest Shaligram in Muktinath, supposedly over 170 million years old and weighing 30 tonnes, a claim amplified by Zee News and several social media users. A fact-check by Alt News found it misleading: Himalaya Times and Online Khabar reports from June 30, 2026, showed the same 22-foot-tall Shaligram sculpture in Mustang district was carved by artistes from the Nepal Academy of Fine Arts from a large rock on the Kali Gandaki’s banks in Jomsom between April 20 and May 4 and unveiled on May 28, with geolocation confirming it stands opposite Thakkhola Lodge & Jimihendrix Restaurant in Jomsom.

#### Iran targets Kuwait in retaliation for US bombardments

**In short:** Iran fired at Kuwait on Thursday as part of its retaliation for US bombardments earlier in the week, with Kuwait's Ministry of Defence saying sirens sounded and air defences intercepted missiles and drones during "blatant Iranian aggression." The escalation followed US strikes on Iranian rocket launchers on an island in the Strait of Hormuz on Sunday, while Israeli police separately arrested a settler suspected in a West Bank attack on Palestinians and mourners buried two Palestinians aged 16 and 19 killed by Israeli forces in Al-Mughayyir.

#### Rubio says no India-Iran trade deal

**In short:** US Secretary of State Marco Rubio said he did not think India and Iran were talking of a trade deal, telling The Brian Kilmeade Show on Fox News Radio that countries develop their own foreign policy and many nations around the world talk to the Iranian regime. His remarks came in response to Prime Minister Narendra Modi's meeting with Iranian President Massoud Pezeshkian on the sidelines of the Shanghai Cooperation Organisation Summit, and Rubio said President Donald Trump has made clear that no country should help Iran evade sanctions.

#### Russia to shutter German cultural centres in tit-for-tat

**In short:** Russia will shutter German cultural centres across the country in a tit-for-tat measure, Foreign Minister Sergey Lavrov said Thursday, after Berlin blamed Moscow for an attempted attack at Leipzig/Halle Airport and announced it was closing the Russian Consulate in Bonn and the Russian House cultural centre in Berlin. The move reflects soured relations and deepening tensions between Moscow and European countries helping Ukraine resist Russia's invasion amid repeated Western accusations of Russian sabotage campaigns.

#### Chinese official warns of more glacier collapses

**In short:** A senior Chinese climate official warned that glaciers on the Tibetan Plateau are likely to become more unstable, potentially leading to more collapses and disasters similar to the August 26 ice-rock avalanche near the Nepal-Tibet border that devastated towns and villages in northern and central Nepal and killed hundreds. Gao Rong, deputy director of China's National Climate Centre, said glacier area on the plateau has shrunk by around 24 per cent over the past 60 years, with around 7,000 small glaciers disappearing completely.

#### PM calls on B'deshis to protect existing harmony

**In short:** Prime Minister Tarique Rahman called on Bangladeshis to remain vigilant against anti-social forces trying to destroy the "existing atmosphere of harmony" in the country, speaking at a state-sponsored Janmashtami reception and honorarium programme for prominent Hindu community members, priests and temple custodians at Osmani Memorial Auditorium in Dhaka. Rahman said people of different faiths have lived together in Bangladesh for generations, maintaining mutual respect and harmony.

#### South Asian American lawmakers seek concrete steps to fight hate

**In short:** A group of South Asian American elected representatives called for concrete steps to combat hate and prevent violence at a roundtable of officials of South Asian origin convened by Indian American Impact and Stop AAPI Hate at the US Capitol on Wednesday. US Congresspersons Raja Krishnamoorthi, Shri Thanedar, Ami Bera, Suhas Subramanyam and Pramila Jayapal attended, discussing strategies to strengthen prevention, reporting and response, including Krishnamoorthi's work on the Hate Crimes Commission Act.

#### 2 dead, suspect killed in downtown Minneapolis

**In short:** Two people were killed and the suspect is dead after a shooting in a downtown Minneapolis apartment building on Wednesday afternoon, police said. Three Minneapolis police officers were also injured, including two from gunshot wounds, though all three were reported in stable condition at Hennepin County Medical Center after responding to a 911 call about shots fired just after 4:30 pm.

#### Putin cites chance of peace deal with Ukraine

**In short:** Russian President Vladimir Putin said on Thursday there was a chance of reaching an agreement to end the war in Ukraine, while Ukrainian Foreign Minister Andrii Sybiha said he expected a "new dynamic" in peace efforts. Speaking at an economic forum in Vladivostok, Putin said Russia and Ukraine must ultimately resolve the conflict, and in Kyiv Indian Foreign Minister S Jaishankar offered India's help in any manner if useful, recalling Prime Minister Narendra Modi's call in Bishkek for endless war to give way to the end of war.

#### El Nino could be strongest in decades, warns UN

**In short:** The World Meteorological Organization warned on Thursday that El Nino is forecast to be the strongest since comparable records began four decades ago and is near certain to persist through February 2027. WMO chief Celeste Saulo said if the trajectory continues it may be stronger than anything since monitoring began, with the phenomenon set to peak towards the end of this year and knock-on climate impacts continuing well into 2027.

#### Meloni's govt sets Italian longevity record

**In short:** Meloni's conservative administration became Italy's longest-serving uninterrupted government since World War II at 1,413 days on Thursday, surpassing the record set by the late Silvio Berlusconi between 2001 and 2005. Meloni and her party planned to celebrate the feat on Friday in Bari.

#### Nepal flood death toll reaches 1,259

**In short:** Devastating floods on the China-Nepal border have killed at least 1,259 people in Nepal with 5,083 missing, while Chinese state media reported 21 killed in Tibet and 541 missing, taking the total toll from the August 26 disaster to 1,280 dead and 5,624 missing. Indian Ambassador Naveen Srivastava visited Syabrubesi and said Indian and Nepali teams have explored 100 metres inside the Chilime Power House tunnel using special equipment and will continue rescue work undeterred despite difficult terrain.

#### Feminist icon Gloria Steinem dies at 92

**In short:** Gloria Steinem, the celebrated author and activist who became one of the most visible symbols and potent voices of the US women's movement and a co-founder of Ms Magazine, died on Wednesday at her home in New York City at 92. Much of her life as an advocate was spent on the road speaking at college campuses, community centres, rallies and marches, and she credited a 1969 speak-out on abortion rights with changing her career path after beginning as a journalist.

#### Judge blocks Trump's new bid to curb birthright citizenship

**In short:** US District Judge Deborah L Boardman in Maryland granted a preliminary injunction blocking President Donald Trump's latest executive order targeting "birth tourism" until a class-action lawsuit brought by immigrant families and advocacy groups is resolved. Boardman wrote that "The Supreme Court has spoken: Children in the certified class are citizens at birth," noting birthright citizenship is guaranteed under current law for anyone born on US soil, with a few exceptions.

#### Kenya Prez tells Tata Chemicals to end ops

**In short:** Kenyan President William Ruto on Thursday ordered Tata Chemicals to stop its operations in Kenya, saying its presence had failed to benefit the country. Ruto said the government would bring in two new companies to take over operations.

Kenyan President William Ruto on Thursday ordered Tata Chemicals to stop its operations in Kenya, saying its presence had failed to benefit the country. Ruto said the government would bring in two new companies to take over operations. In late July, Tata had said Kenya ordered Tata Chemicals to suspend operations at the Magadi Soda factory and suspended exports of soda ash.

#### Riots spread to Cape Town; march by ‘coloureds’ (FROM THE ARCHIVES 1976)

**In short:** Cape Town was rocked for a second day on September 3, 1976 by racial disturbances after about 3,000 coloured youths erupted in violence in the white centre, with unconfirmed reports that 5,000 coloured youths were marching from outlying black townships. Police opened fire at Trafalgar High School and with shotguns at demonstrators in the city centre, killing one person and wounding at least two others, while four people had been killed in disturbances in the city the previous day.

#### 'Supersized' El Nino to be strongest ever recorded: UN

**In short:** The World Meteorological Organisation said on Thursday that El Nino is set to intensify into 2027 and could be the strongest ever recorded, with near 100% likelihood of persistence through February 2027 fuelled by exceptionally warm Pacific Ocean temperatures—the first time WMO has expressed such certainty. Secretary-General Celeste Saulo said if the trajectory continues it may be stronger than anything since monitoring began, potentially shifting global rainfall and temperature patterns and fuelling droughts and typhoons as WMO steps up preparedness efforts.

#### Iran attacks U.S. bases in West Asia despite Trump's threats of strikes

**In short:** Iran attacked U.S. military bases in Kuwait on Thursday despite President Donald Trump threatening further strikes six months into renewed conflict, with Tehran retaliating across West Asia after U.S.

Iran attacked U.S. military bases in Kuwait on Thursday despite President Donald Trump threatening further strikes six months into renewed conflict, with Tehran retaliating across West Asia after U.S. bombardment left at least 19 Iranians dead between Tuesday and Wednesday. Iran’s army said it hit Ahmad al-Jaber Air Base in Kuwait with missiles and drones while Kuwait intercepted the attack and condemned it as a threat to security and a grave breach of international law; shrapnel from a U.S. strike on a wedding in Kuhestak killed four including a child and wounded more than 50.

#### The feminist organiser and her everyday rebellions

**In short:** Gloria Steinem (1934–2026), described on her website as a “feminist organizer,” is remembered for movement-building through Talking Circles at her New York home and decades of activism including founding Ms. magazine and chairing Apne Aap Women Worldwide’s advisory board.

Gloria Steinem (1934–2026), described on her website as a “feminist organizer,” is remembered for movement-building through Talking Circles at her New York home and decades of activism including founding Ms. magazine and chairing Apne Aap Women Worldwide’s advisory board. Steinem, who spent two years in India after graduating Smith College in 1956 and co-edited As if Women Matter with Ruchira Gupta, wrote nine books, reframed abortion as “reproductive freedom,” and influenced feminist thought globally; colleagues recall her India ties, her Kali cover on Ms.’s first issue selling 3 lakh copies, and her humour and accessibility until her death.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** feminist organiser and her everyday rebellions

**Important points**
