#### Foreign arms, drones fuel Sudan war, says UN probe

**In short:** A UN fact-finding mission said on Thursday that foreign weapons, military technology, fighters and logistical networks are sustaining Sudan’s conflict and expanding warring parties’ ability to strike distant civilian areas with drones. Investigators found grounds to believe a network involving individuals and entities in the UAE, Chad, Libya and Somalia supplied the RSF with weapons, equipment, logistics, training and foreign personnel, while external support for Sudan’s military was at an early stage.

#### Alcaraz shrugs off early setback to forge ahead

**In short:** Defending U.S. Open champion Carlos Alcaraz rebounded from dropping the first set to beat Jaime Faria 4-6, 6-0, 6-3, 6-2 on Wednesday night in New York, winning eight straight games after a mid-first-set break.

Defending U.S. Open champion Carlos Alcaraz rebounded from dropping the first set to beat Jaime Faria 4-6, 6-0, 6-3, 6-2 on Wednesday night in New York, winning eight straight games after a mid-first-set break. The No. 2 seed, returning from a four-month right wrist injury that sidelined him from the French Open and Wimbledon, said four sets helped test his body and that he is no longer in pain. Alcaraz is on a 16-match Grand Slam winning streak with seven major titles and did not drop a set until last year's final against Jannik Sinner; Daniil Medvedev called him a top title favourite despite uncertainty over how his wrist will hold up.

#### Aryna powers her way into 3rd round of US Open in just 53 min

**In short:** Top-seeded Aryna Sabalenka beat Polina Iatcenko 6-1, 6-1 in 53 minutes on Wednesday to reach the U.S. Open third round, looking ready to pursue a third straight Flushing Meadows title after recent fourth-round exits.

Top-seeded Aryna Sabalenka beat Polina Iatcenko 6-1, 6-1 in 53 minutes on Wednesday to reach the U.S. Open third round, looking ready to pursue a third straight Flushing Meadows title after recent fourth-round exits. Rain suspended play on outside courts while matches under Arthur Ashe and Louis Armstrong roofs continued; Jessica Pegula beat Sofia Kenin 6-3, 6-1 in 56 minutes, No. 8 Ben Shelton defeated Hubert Hurkacz 6-3, 5-7, 7-6 (3), 7-5, and No. 7 Daniil Medvedev beat Sebastian Gorzny 6-3, 6-3, 7-5 after a brief delay when two birds sat on the net. Sabalenka won 15 of 16 first-serve points in the opening set and went 21 for 28 on first serve in the match.

#### Naqvi hits back at Pak cricket critics

**In short:** PCB Chairman Mohsin Naqvi, speaking in London, lashed out at criticism of Pakistan's Test team after 0-2 losses to England, saying detractors "have never achieved anything in their lives." He cited other teams' losses to weaker opponents, including India's recent T20 defeats to Ireland, to argue setbacks do not define a side. Naqvi and the PCB faced fire after replacing seven players in the England tour squad with mostly young names in the three-match series.

#### BCCI leaves Rohit's future up in the air

**In short:** The BCCI performance review meeting on Thursday did little to clarify Rohit Sharma’s ODI future, with secretary Devajit Saikia brushing aside speculation by citing Rohit’s 138 in the July 19 Lord’s ODI against England and stressing it was not a selection meeting focused instead on England, Ireland and the 2027 World Cup roadmap. The Hindu understands the selection committee had told Rohit during the June Chennai ODI against Afghanistan he was not in plans beyond the England series, but BCCI administrators overruled that before Lord’s; with chief selector Ajit Agarkar absent, the question was deliberately left unresolved.

#### Asian Games: Yashvir comes in for Neeraj

**In short:** Commonwealth Games bronze medallist javelin thrower Yashvir Singh has been named Neeraj Chopra's replacement in the men's javelin for the upcoming Asian Games as per the latest 77-member Asiad team released by the Athletics Federation of India. Neeraj prematurely ended his season due to an ankle ligament tear after securing silver at the Glasgow Commonwealth Games and recording 88.05m at the Lausanne Diamond League, and Abhishek Pal's name is missing from the list after the distance runner reportedly tested positive for a banned substance.

#### Smriti scales the summit as India enters semifinals

**In short:** Smriti Mandhana’s 64-ball 124 (14 fours, seven sixes) powered India to 193 for five and a 137-run win over Hong Kong in Dubai, sealing a Women’s Asia Cup semifinal berth as Hong Kong were bowled out for 56. Smriti surpassed Mithali Raj’s 10,868 runs to become the leading run-getter in women’s internationals with 10,880, went past Meg Lanning and Laura Wolvaardt for most hundreds (18) across formats, and became the first Indian woman with two T20I centuries; India next face Pakistan on September 5.

### Lifestyle

#### MULETHI for hair growth

**In short:** Mulethi (Glycyrrhiza glabra), or licorice root, is an Ayurvedic botanical rich in glycyrrhizin, flavonoids, and phytoestrogens that soothes inflamed scalps, combats Malassezia yeast behind dandruff, improves microcirculation to follicles, and inhibits 5-alpha-reductase to reduce DHT-linked hair thinning, while its mucilage helps retain moisture and reduce breakage. Home remedies include a weekly mulethi–curd–honey mask (2 tablespoons mulethi powder, 3–4 tablespoons curd, 1 teaspoon honey, left on 30–45 minutes), a mulethi-infused coconut or sesame oil scalp massage 1–2 times weekly, and a mulethi–amla scalp spray boiled in water and applied after washing without rinsing.

Mulethi (Glycyrrhiza glabra), or licorice root, is an Ayurvedic botanical rich in glycyrrhizin, flavonoids, and phytoestrogens that soothes inflamed scalps, combats Malassezia yeast behind dandruff, improves microcirculation to follicles, and inhibits 5-alpha-reductase to reduce DHT-linked hair thinning, while its mucilage helps retain moisture and reduce breakage. Home remedies include a weekly mulethi–curd–honey mask (2 tablespoons mulethi powder, 3–4 tablespoons curd, 1 teaspoon honey, left on 30–45 minutes), a mulethi-infused coconut or sesame oil scalp massage 1–2 times weekly, and a mulethi–amla scalp spray boiled in water and applied after washing without rinsing. Users should patch-test before full application and rinse thoroughly to avoid gritty residue on light-colored hair.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** MULETHI for hair growth
- **Told:** Mulethi (Glycyrrhiza glabra), or licorice root, is an Ayurvedic botanical rich in glycyrrhizin, flavonoids, and phytoestrogens that soothes inflamed scalps, combats Malassezia yeast behind dandruff, improves microcirculation to follicles, and inhibits 5-alpha-reductase to reduce DHT-linked hair thinning, while its mucilage helps retain moisture and reduce breakage.
- **Purpose:** Users should patch-test before full application and rinse thoroughly to avoid gritty residue on light-colored hair.

**Important points**
- Users should patch-test before full application and rinse thoroughly to avoid gritty residue on light-colored hair.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.8_

#### Understanding pineapple, bromelain and cancer

**In short:** Pineapple (Ananas comosus) is rich in vitamin C, manganese, dietary fiber, and antioxidants, and contains bromelain—proteolytic enzymes concentrated mainly in the stem and juice that laboratory research has studied for anti-inflammatory and antitumor effects. Purified bromelain extracts in cell and animal studies can induce apoptosis in breast, colon, and melanoma cell lines, inhibit metastasis, reduce inflammatory pathways such as NF-κB and COX-2, and degrade extracellular matrix around solid tumors, but eating whole pineapple yields far lower enzyme doses, bromelain is partially broken down in digestion, and robust human trials proving cancer prevention or treatment are lacking.

Pineapple (Ananas comosus) is rich in vitamin C, manganese, dietary fiber, and antioxidants, and contains bromelain—proteolytic enzymes concentrated mainly in the stem and juice that laboratory research has studied for anti-inflammatory and antitumor effects. Purified bromelain extracts in cell and animal studies can induce apoptosis in breast, colon, and melanoma cell lines, inhibit metastasis, reduce inflammatory pathways such as NF-κB and COX-2, and degrade extracellular matrix around solid tumors, but eating whole pineapple yields far lower enzyme doses, bromelain is partially broken down in digestion, and robust human trials proving cancer prevention or treatment are lacking. Pineapple or bromelain supplements must not replace chemotherapy, radiation, or surgery; bromelain can act as a mild blood thinner and interact with anticoagulants such as warfarin, and people allergic to latex, pollen, or papain may react to pineapple or concentrated bromelain.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Understanding pineapple, bromelain and cancer
- **Told:** Pineapple (Ananas comosus) is rich in vitamin C, manganese, dietary fiber, and antioxidants, and contains bromelain—proteolytic enzymes concentrated mainly in the stem and juice that laboratory research has studied for anti-inflammatory and antitumor effects.
- **Purpose:** Pineapple or bromelain supplements must not replace chemotherapy, radiation, or surgery; bromelain can act as a mild blood thinner and interact with anticoagulants such as warfarin, and people allergic to latex, pollen, or papain may react to pineapple or concentrated bromelain.

**Important points**
- Pineapple or bromelain supplements must not replace chemotherapy, radiation, or surgery.
- bromelain can act as a mild blood thinner and interact with anticoagulants such as warfarin, and people allergic to latex, pollen, or papain may react to pineapple or concentrated bromelain.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.8_

#### Understanding systemic lupus erythematosus

**In short:** Systemic Lupus Erythematosus (SLE), commonly known as lupus, is a complex chronic autoimmune disorder in which the immune system loses tolerance and produces autoantibodies that target healthy tissues, causing variable flares and remission across organ systems. It is driven by genetic predisposition (over 100 linked loci, including HLA-DR2 and HLA-DR3), environmental triggers such as UV light, Epstein-Barr virus, certain drugs, and stress, and hormonal factors—roughly 90% of patients are women aged 15–44.

Systemic Lupus Erythematosus (SLE), commonly known as lupus, is a complex chronic autoimmune disorder in which the immune system loses tolerance and produces autoantibodies that target healthy tissues, causing variable flares and remission across organ systems. It is driven by genetic predisposition (over 100 linked loci, including HLA-DR2 and HLA-DR3), environmental triggers such as UV light, Epstein-Barr virus, certain drugs, and stress, and hormonal factors—roughly 90% of patients are women aged 15–44. Symptoms range from profound fatigue, low-grade fever, and a malar “butterfly” rash to photosensitivity, discoid lesions, Raynaud’s phenomenon, and non-erosive arthritis in over 90% of patients; diagnosis relies on medical history, ACR/EULAR criteria, ANA testing (positive in over 95%), specific autoantibodies including Anti-dsDNA and Anti-Sm, and complement levels C3 and C4.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Understanding systemic lupus erythematosus
- **Told:** Systemic Lupus Erythematosus (SLE), commonly known as lupus, is a complex chronic autoimmune disorder in which the immune system loses tolerance and produces autoantibodies that target healthy tissues, causing variable flares and remission across organ systems.
- **Purpose:** Symptoms range from profound fatigue, low-grade fever, and a malar “butterfly” rash to photosensitivity, discoid lesions, Raynaud’s phenomenon, and non-erosive arthritis in over 90% of patients; diagnosis relies on medical history, ACR/EULAR criteria, ANA testing (positive in over 95%), specific autoantibodies including Anti-dsDNA and Anti-Sm, and complement levels C3 and C4.

**Important points**
- Symptoms range from profound fatigue, low-grade fever, and a malar “butterfly” rash to photosensitivity, discoid lesions, Raynaud’s phenomenon, and non-erosive arthritis in over 90% of patients.
- diagnosis relies on medical history, ACR/EULAR criteria, ANA testing (positive in over 95%), specific autoantibodies including Anti-dsDNA and Anti-Sm, and complement levels C3 and C4.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.8_

#### Here's why the soil is BROWN

**In short:** Soil gets its rich brown colour from a blend of decomposed plant and animal matter (humus) and weathered minerals, primarily iron. When leaves, roots, and tiny organisms die, soil microbes digest them into humus, which is rich in carbon and gives topsoil a deep brown-to-black shade; oxidized iron from earth’s crust meeting oxygen and water creates yellow, red, and warm brown mineral tints.

Soil gets its rich brown colour from a blend of decomposed plant and animal matter (humus) and weathered minerals, primarily iron. When leaves, roots, and tiny organisms die, soil microbes digest them into humus, which is rich in carbon and gives topsoil a deep brown-to-black shade; oxidized iron from earth’s crust meeting oxygen and water creates yellow, red, and warm brown mineral tints. Soil colour varies with local climate, drainage, and biological activity—dark brown or black indicates high humus and fertility, bright red or orange points to high iron oxides and low organic matter, yellow forms when iron hydrates, gray or blue-green occurs in waterlogged conditions, and white or pale soil signals salt, quartz sand, or leached nutrients.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Here's why the soil is BROWN
- **Told:** Soil gets its rich brown colour from a blend of decomposed plant and animal matter (humus) and weathered minerals, primarily iron.
- **Purpose:** Soil colour varies with local climate, drainage, and biological activity—dark brown or black indicates high humus and fertility, bright red or orange points to high iron oxides and low organic matter, yellow forms when iron hydrates, gray or blue-green occurs in waterlogged conditions, and white or pale soil signals salt, quartz sand, or leached nutrients.

**Important points**
- Soil colour varies with local climate, drainage, and biological activity—dark brown or black indicates high humus and fertility, bright red or orange points to high iron oxides and low organic matter, yellow forms when iron hydrates, gray or blue-green occurs in waterlogged conditions, and white or pale soil signals salt, quartz sand, or leached nutrients.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.10_

#### Lessons from a jam

**In short:** Traffic jams expose human impatience and can teach management lessons while waiting for traffic to move. Management consultant Vriddhi Chawla suggests finding the actual bottleneck before asking people to work faster, noting that a designer waiting for content, a content team waiting for approval, or an approver stuck in another meeting may be the real blocker; HR consultant Riddhima Baweja warns that adding more people to a struggling project can create chaos and pull pace down without clear directions; Chawla also stresses that people should know who is responsible for what and how communication hierarchy works; psychologist Vijaya notes patience when things do not move at the desired pace, and the takeaway is that good management requires clear communication, giving people space, avoiding unnecessary changes, and sometimes waiting strategically.

#### What your routine says

**In short:** It is known that daily routine strongly reflects what matters to us and defines our priorities, making it important to have a routine that keeps you connected with the right priorities. Dr Prajakta Gupte, Consultant Psychiatry at Jupiter Hospital, Thane, says priorities are visible in where time, energy, and attention go—starting the day with exercise and balanced meals suggests health and self-discipline rank high, while a routine dominated by work, constant notifications, and little rest suggests productivity over well-being; business and life coach Sanjuri Ray calls daily routines an honest record of what really matters, warning that a day run by whoever messages first can quietly rewrite identity.

It is known that daily routine strongly reflects what matters to us and defines our priorities, making it important to have a routine that keeps you connected with the right priorities. Dr Prajakta Gupte, Consultant Psychiatry at Jupiter Hospital, Thane, says priorities are visible in where time, energy, and attention go—starting the day with exercise and balanced meals suggests health and self-discipline rank high, while a routine dominated by work, constant notifications, and little rest suggests productivity over well-being; business and life coach Sanjuri Ray calls daily routines an honest record of what really matters, warning that a day run by whoever messages first can quietly rewrite identity. Seven habit areas—first 30 minutes of the morning, how and where you eat, spending patterns, free time use, phone behaviour, reading between routine lines, and whether others notice your routine—reveal what you truly prioritise.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** What your routine says
- **Told:** It is known that daily routine strongly reflects what matters to us and defines our priorities, making it important to have a routine that keeps you connected with the right priorities.
- **Purpose:** Seven habit areas—first 30 minutes of the morning, how and where you eat, spending patterns, free time use, phone behaviour, reading between routine lines, and whether others notice your routine—reveal what you truly prioritise.

**Important points**

_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.10_

#### Left on read?

**In short:** In the age of instant communication, even a few hours of silence can feel significant, and a delayed reply may trigger uncertainty, overthinking, or anxiety despite having little to do with the relationship itself. Clinical psychologist Elza Dsouza explains that without tone or facial expressions, silence can feel like rejection, and the key is to separate facts from interpretations; Gen Z content creator Snehal D’Souza describes hesitating to double-text for fear of seeming too eager or desperate when left on read; Mumbai counsellor Deepal Mehta says a delayed reply becomes a blank canvas for fears, where checking online status and rereading messages can reinforce anxiety, and that a delayed response is a fact while what we believe it means is an assumption.

#### THE HEALTH QUIZ

**In short:** National Eye Donation fortnight, observed annually in India from August 25 to September 8 to raise awareness about corneal donation, is marked with a six-question quiz on eye health covering check-up frequency, Dr. Amar Agarwal's corneal technique, diabetic retinopathy stages, cornea viability after spectacle use, the 20-20-20 rule, and timing of cornea removal after death; answers may be sent to health@thehindu.co.in.

#### THE DAILY QUIZ

**In short:** Gloria Steinem passed away yesterday at the age of 92, and the daily quiz tests readers on the feminist magazine she co-founded in 1971, her remark about already having "one child," Betty Friedan's book credited with launching the second wave of feminism, the actor who co-founded with her and Robin Morgan an organisation to make women visible in media, and a controversial op-ed defending a U.S. President's conduct with women.

#### Know your English

**In short:** From the archives of November 13, 2001, S. Upendran explains that "bolt from the blue" or "bolt out of the blue" means something sudden and totally unexpected, that "vox populi" or "vox pop" is Latin for "voice of the people" and denotes street interviews used in broadcasting, that "plenty" and "aplenty" both mean large amounts but "aplenty" is old-fashioned and usually follows the noun, and that both "motherland" and "fatherland" are acceptable depending on language and culture.

#### Inside the mind of Singeetham Srinivasa Rao: a masterclass in storytelling

**In short:** Sudhish Kamath profiles the 95-year-old filmmaker as an instinctive writer whose films from Sommokadidhi Sokokadidhi and Pushpak to Aditya 369 often place characters in worlds where they do not belong, drawing on mentors such as K.V. Reddy — who taught him to write "dark cave" not just "cave" — and on contrast and musical narration for comedy and economy.

Sudhish Kamath profiles the 95-year-old filmmaker as an instinctive writer whose films from Sommokadidhi Sokokadidhi and Pushpak to Aditya 369 often place characters in worlds where they do not belong, drawing on mentors such as K.V. Reddy — who taught him to write "dark cave" not just "cave" — and on contrast and musical narration for comedy and economy. Rao, who recently released Sing Geetham, serves as story consultant on Kalki 2898 AD and is working on a historical spy thriller, says collaboration not negotiation defines his long partnership with Kamal Haasan and that turning any proverb into a modern story can help young writers find their voice.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Inside the mind of Singeetham Srinivasa Rao: a masterclass in storytelling

#### 1. ChatGPT, Gemini, Claude face outages; AWS also affected

**In short:** Major artificial intelligence platforms, including OpenAI’s ChatGPT, Google Gemini and Anthropic’s Claude, experienced significant service disruptions on Thursday, affecting thousands of users globally, according to outage tracking website Downdetector. The disruption also appeared to impact Grok and Amazon Web Services, with users reporting website inaccessibility and internal server errors.

Major artificial intelligence platforms, including OpenAI’s ChatGPT, Google Gemini and Anthropic’s Claude, experienced significant service disruptions on Thursday, affecting thousands of users globally, according to outage tracking website Downdetector. The disruption also appeared to impact Grok and Amazon Web Services, with users reporting website inaccessibility and internal server errors. OpenAI said it had applied a mitigation and was monitoring recovery; Anthropic later said only Opus 4.8 and Opus 5 models remained affected.

#### 2. Indian envoys explore global partnerships in tech, investment, innovation in K'taka

**In short:** Indian envoys to the US and Azerbaijan visited Karnataka and held high-level meetings with the governor, chief minister and senior ministers, exploring opportunities to expand economic, technological, investment and institutional partnerships, the Consulate General of India in Los Angeles said. Consul General of India in Los Angeles Ambassador K J Srinivasa and Ambassador of India to Azerbaijan Abhay Kumar called on Governor Thaawarchand Gehlot and Chief Minister D K Shivakumar and met Ministers Priyank Kharge, M B Patil, K J George and Byrathi Suresh.

#### 3. Human Rights body seeks reports on Insta ads linked to child sex abuse

**In short:** The National Human Rights Commission has directed the Ministry of Electronics and Information Technology, the Ministry of Information and Broadcasting and the Delhi Police to submit point-wise action-taken reports within two weeks in a case involving paid Instagram advertisements that allegedly facilitated access to child sexual abuse material. The NHRC bench led by Priyank Kanoongo took cognisance after BBC World Service reports alleged that paid advertisements using terms like 'rape video' and 'child video' bypassed Meta's review mechanisms and directed users to Telegram channels where the material was allegedly available for sale.

The National Human Rights Commission has directed the Ministry of Electronics and Information Technology, the Ministry of Information and Broadcasting and the Delhi Police to submit point-wise action-taken reports within two weeks in a case involving paid Instagram advertisements that allegedly facilitated access to child sexual abuse material. The NHRC bench led by Priyank Kanoongo took cognisance after BBC World Service reports alleged that paid advertisements using terms like 'rape video' and 'child video' bypassed Meta's review mechanisms and directed users to Telegram channels where the material was allegedly available for sale. The commission called for a probe under the Pocso Act, 2012, the Information Technology Act, 2000, and the intermediary framework, along with preservation of electronic evidence and rescue and rehabilitation of child victims.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Human Rights body seeks reports on Insta ads linked to child sex abuse
- **Told:** The National Human Rights Commission has directed the Ministry of Electronics and Information Technology, the Ministry of Information and Broadcasting and the Delhi Police to submit point-wise action-taken reports within two weeks in a case involving paid Instagram advertisements that allegedly facilitated access to child sexual abuse material.
- **Purpose:** The commission called for a probe under the Pocso Act, 2012, the Information Technology Act, 2000, and the intermediary framework, along with preservation of electronic evidence and rescue and rehabilitation of child victims.

**Important points**
- The commission called for a probe under the Pocso Act, 2012, the Information Technology Act, 2000, and the intermediary framework, along with preservation of electronic evidence and rescue and rehabilitation of child victims.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.3_

#### 4. Mamdani frowns on AI use for New York students; imposes 1-yr ban

**In short:** New York City public schools will temporarily bar elementary and middle school students from using generative artificial intelligence tools during the upcoming school year, Mayor Zohran Mamdani said on Wednesday, in what officials described as the most expansive such prohibition in the United States. The one-year moratorium bars student-facing generative AI software through eighth grade, affecting roughly 600,000 public school students, halts use of about 40 educational AI tools, and bans companion chatbots across all grade levels, while high schoolers are exempt from the full ban.

New York City public schools will temporarily bar elementary and middle school students from using generative artificial intelligence tools during the upcoming school year, Mayor Zohran Mamdani said on Wednesday, in what officials described as the most expansive such prohibition in the United States. The one-year moratorium bars student-facing generative AI software through eighth grade, affecting roughly 600,000 public school students, halts use of about 40 educational AI tools, and bans companion chatbots across all grade levels, while high schoolers are exempt from the full ban. The plan also includes recommended screen time limits of 30 minutes daily for grades three to five and 45 minutes for grades six to eight.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Mamdani frowns on AI use for New York students; imposes 1-yr ban
- **Told:** New York City public schools will temporarily bar elementary and middle school students from using generative artificial intelligence tools during the upcoming school year, Mayor Zohran Mamdani said on Wednesday, in what officials described as the most expansive such prohibition in the United States.
- **Purpose:** The plan also includes recommended screen time limits of 30 minutes daily for grades three to five and 45 minutes for grades six to eight.

**Important points**
- The plan also includes recommended screen time limits of 30 minutes daily for grades three to five and 45 minutes for grades six to eight.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.1_

#### 5. Carver Aviation keen to set up flying training academy

**In short:** Carver Aviation, based in Baramati, Maharashtra, has evinced interest in setting up a satellite flying training academy at Vijayapura Airport, which is ready for inauguration, Karnataka Infrastructure Development Minister M B Patil said on Wednesday. The company has sought 2.5 acres of land adjoining the airport premises; if implemented, the project will facilitate flight training, aircraft maintenance, repair and overhaul, and ground support activities.

### More technology

#### Silicon Valley's big money is about to get a lot bigger

**In short:** As Anthropic and OpenAI prepare to go public, at least 95 investors have put money into both companies, according to PitchBook — a break from the old venture-capital norm of backing only one rival in a category. Anthropic has raised more than $130 billion from roughly 300 investors and OpenAI more than $180 billion from roughly 230, dwarfing Facebook's $2.4 billion and Uber's roughly $20 billion before their IPOs; Anthropic's listing could value it at $2 trillion.

As Anthropic and OpenAI prepare to go public, at least 95 investors have put money into both companies, according to PitchBook — a break from the old venture-capital norm of backing only one rival in a category. Anthropic has raised more than $130 billion from roughly 300 investors and OpenAI more than $180 billion from roughly 230, dwarfing Facebook's $2.4 billion and Uber's roughly $20 billion before their IPOs; Anthropic's listing could value it at $2 trillion. Top Sand Hill Road firms have adapted with special-purpose vehicles — Menlo Ventures rounded small investors into a $750 million entity for Anthropic — and even Sequoia Capital, which had earlier passed on Anthropic while backing OpenAI and xAI, joined two financing rounds this year including one in May valuing the company at $900 billion.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Silicon Valley's big money is about to get a lot bigger
- **Told:** As Anthropic and OpenAI prepare to go public, at least 95 investors have put money into both companies, according to PitchBook — a break from the old venture-capital norm of backing only one rival in a category.
- **Purpose:** Top Sand Hill Road firms have adapted with special-purpose vehicles — Menlo Ventures rounded small investors into a $750 million entity for Anthropic — and even Sequoia Capital, which had earlier passed on Anthropic while backing OpenAI and xAI, joined two financing rounds this year including one in May valuing the company at $900 billion.

**Important points**
- Top Sand Hill Road firms have adapted with special-purpose vehicles — Menlo Ventures rounded small investors into a $750 million entity for Anthropic — and even Sequoia Capital, which had earlier passed on Anthropic while backing OpenAI and xAI, joined two financing rounds this year including one in May valuing the company at $900 billion.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.9_

#### Govt plans social media code for employees

**In short:** Amid a growing trend of government staff posting reels, videos and personal opinions on platforms such as X, Facebook and Instagram, the Centre is working on a social media code of conduct for its officers and employees. The proposal has been deliberated at the highest levels of government and is expected to apply across ranks, though the timeline and specific provisions are not yet known.

Amid a growing trend of government staff posting reels, videos and personal opinions on platforms such as X, Facebook and Instagram, the Centre is working on a social media code of conduct for its officers and employees. The proposal has been deliberated at the highest levels of government and is expected to apply across ranks, though the timeline and specific provisions are not yet known. The move comes amid broader efforts to tighten online content regulation, including reducing the time available to social media platforms to act on sensitive content from 24 hours to two hours under amendments to the Information Technology Rules, 2021, notified on February 10.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Govt plans social media code for employees
- **Told:** Amid a growing trend of government staff posting reels, videos and personal opinions on platforms such as X, Facebook and Instagram, the Centre is working on a social media code of conduct for its officers and employees.
- **Purpose:** The move comes amid broader efforts to tighten online content regulation, including reducing the time available to social media platforms to act on sensitive content from 24 hours to two hours under amendments to the Information Technology Rules, 2021, notified on February 10.

**Important points**
- The move comes amid broader efforts to tighten online content regulation, including reducing the time available to social media platforms to act on sensitive content from 24 hours to two hours under amendments to the Information Technology Rules, 2021, notified on February 10.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.11_

#### NVIDIA acquires AI startup Hugging Face for $12.9 billion

**In short:** NVIDIA on Thursday said it has acquired AI startup Hugging Face for $12.9 billion. CEO Jensen Huang said the acquisition will scale Hugging Face’s platform, strengthen its infrastructure and expand access to AI for developers and institutions worldwide; more than 18 million developers, researchers and creators use Hugging Face to share more than 3 million models.

NVIDIA on Thursday said it has acquired AI startup Hugging Face for $12.9 billion. CEO Jensen Huang said the acquisition will scale Hugging Face’s platform, strengthen its infrastructure and expand access to AI for developers and institutions worldwide; more than 18 million developers, researchers and creators use Hugging Face to share more than 3 million models. Huang said Hugging Face will remain an open platform and NVIDIA compute will not be required to build on or deploy through Hugging Face.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** NVIDIA acquires AI startup Hugging Face for $12.9 billion
- **Told:** NVIDIA on Thursday said it has acquired AI startup Hugging Face for $12.9 billion.
- **Purpose:** Huang said Hugging Face will remain an open platform and NVIDIA compute will not be required to build on or deploy through Hugging Face.

**Important points**
- Huang said Hugging Face will remain an open platform and NVIDIA compute will not be required to build on or deploy through Hugging Face.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.12_

## Opinion & Explainers

### When oil becomes a weapon, India must fuel its way (Opinion)

**In short:** The latest US-Iran confrontation underscores India's vulnerability to imported crude oil dependence, and ethanol-blended petrol is framed as an energy-security imperative beyond environmental policy. India achieved its 20% ethanol-blending target in 2025-26, up from less than 1.5% in 2013-14, with ethanol production capacity expanding nearly fivefold since 2014.

- The latest US-Iran confrontation underscores India's vulnerability to imported crude oil dependence, and ethanol-blended petrol is framed as an energy-security imperative beyond environmental policy.
- India achieved its 20% ethanol-blending target in 2025-26, up from less than 1.5% in 2013-14, with ethanol production capacity expanding nearly fivefold since 2014.
- The analysis argues E20 should be a springboard for broader transport-fuel diversification while managing concerns over water consumption, feedstock availability, food security, vehicle compatibility, and consumer choice.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.1_

### Waiving tuition fee for SC, ST students (Opinion)

**In short:** The Odisha government’s decision to waive admission fees for Scheduled Caste and Scheduled Tribe students securing seats in State-run medical and engineering colleges addresses a financial barrier in higher education, the writer argues, but institutions should also extend subsidised accommodation, accessible study materials, and mentorship to ensure sustainable academic success. States across the country should emulate such targeted welfare measures while strengthening foundational schooling to level the playing field early.

- The Odisha government’s decision to waive admission fees for Scheduled Caste and Scheduled Tribe students securing seats in State-run medical and engineering colleges addresses a financial barrier in higher education, the writer argues, but institutions should also extend subsidised accommodation, accessible study materials, and mentorship to ensure sustainable academic success.
- States across the country should emulate such targeted welfare measures while strengthening foundational schooling to level the playing field early.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.6_

**In short:** A report on a Kargil war veteran attempting suicide citing delay in land allotment made for sad reading, the writer says, asking how battle heroes can be treated this way after sacrificing for the nation’s frontiers. Such issues should grab the government’s immediate attention and be redressed promptly, or people will think poorly of a nation that treats its soldiers in such a shabby manner.

### JCB to bouquet: Protest turns a youthful slip (Opinion)

**In short:** Richard Lloyd writes that after the government and Supreme Court softened toward protesters, FIRs once treated seriously became cases everyone wanted closed, with the government telling the court it had no objection to ending them and the court invoking Article 142 for “complete justice” without investigation or trial, chiefly to protect students’ futures. He asks whether constitutional compassion should depend on a student identity card when farmers, workers, and pensioners also protest, warns that justice must not appear more forgiving depending on who stands before it, and cautions against a model where the State registers an FIR, the government changes its mind, Article 142 is invoked, and everyone goes home happy.

- Richard Lloyd writes that after the government and Supreme Court softened toward protesters.
- FIRs once treated seriously became cases everyone wanted closed, with the government telling the court it had no objection to ending them and the court invoking Article 142 for “complete justice” without investigation or trial, chiefly to protect students’ futures.
- He asks whether constitutional compassion should depend on a student identity card when farmers, workers, and pensioners also protest, warns that justice must not appear more forgiving depending on who stands before it, and cautions against a model where the State registers an FIR, the government changes its mind.
- Article 142 is invoked, and everyone goes home happy.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.6_

### Growth rate is not the whole story (Opinion)

**In short:** India's economy grew 7.8% in the April-June quarter, ahead of RBI forecasts, while nominal GDP grew 10.3%, pointing to investment-led expansion as real gross fixed capital formation rose 11.9% against 7.1% for private final consumption. Deepanshu Mohan argues MoSPI's February GDP rebasing to 2022-23, with methodological changes including double deflation, has revised consumption's share down by about 9-11% over three years while lifting investment's measured share, meaning the headline rate does not tell the full story of who benefits; he calls for prosperity-driven rather than growth-obsessed policy, citing East Asian manufacturing-led models.

- India's economy grew 7.8% in the April-June quarter, ahead of RBI forecasts, while nominal GDP grew 10.3%, pointing to investment-led expansion as real gross fixed capital formation rose 11.9% against 7.1% for private final consumption.
- Deepanshu Mohan argues MoSPI's February GDP rebasing to 2022-23, with methodological changes including double deflation, has revised consumption's share down by about 9-11% over three years while lifting investment's measured share, meaning the headline rate does not tell the full story of who benefits.
- he calls for prosperity-driven rather than growth-obsessed policy, citing East Asian manufacturing-led models.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.8_

### Welcome move (Opinion)

**In short:** At the Union government’s request, the Supreme Court invoked Article 142 to quash criminal cases against youth who joined nationwide Gen Z protests led by the Cockroach Janta Party over examination reforms and government accountability, including demands to withdraw FIRs registered during the July agitations, while directing that no fresh FIRs be filed. The editorial contrasts this relief with the Centre’s handling of largely peaceful farmer protests and anti-CAA demonstrations, where serious charges including terrorism and sedition were invoked and many protesters remain jailed, and argues the Court should extend the same standard to those cases so the CJP relief does not remain an exception.

- At the Union government’s request, the Supreme Court invoked Article 142 to quash criminal cases against youth who joined nationwide Gen Z protests led by the Cockroach Janta Party over examination reforms and government accountability, including demands to withdraw FIRs registered during the July agitations, while directing that no fresh FIRs be filed.
- The editorial contrasts this relief with the Centre’s handling of largely peaceful farmer protests and anti-CAA demonstrations, where serious charges including terrorism and sedition were invoked and many protesters remain jailed, and argues the Court should extend the same standard to those cases so the CJP relief does not remain an exception.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.8_

### Rethink priorities (Opinion)

**In short:** Hemachandra Basappa of Bengaluru, responding to an editorial on private capital driving defence R&D, writes that Indian industry typically imports or assembles under licence rather than invest in R&D, and asks whether India needs a weapons industry or greater people-to-people contact and trade with neighbours; he urges reviving SAARC, open borders or easier regional travel, and stronger contact with South Asian neighbours to transform the region.

- Hemachandra Basappa of Bengaluru, responding to an editorial on private capital driving defence R&D, writes that Indian industry typically imports or assembles under licence rather than invest in R&D, and asks whether India needs a weapons industry or greater people-to-people contact and trade with neighbours.

### Ensure safe food (Opinion)

**In short:** A K Shariff of Mysuru, referring to 34 students falling sick at a Manvi school after breakfast, urges authorities to ensure food supplied to and served in schools and hostels is safe, conduct regular surprise inspections of storage and preparation facilities, and allow parents and stakeholders to visit and report lapses.

### Youth power, an electoral force still in the making (Opinion)

**In short:** Akshay Rout writes that while Gen Z street protests have forced parties to engage with youth issues, India’s largest youth cohort has not yet consolidated into a predictable electoral bloc, with no discernible youth voting trend in recent Assembly polls in Tamil Nadu, Assam, West Bengal, Keralam and Puducherry. About 65% of India’s population is below 35 and roughly 23% of the electorate is aged 18–29, but caste, community and gender identities have largely subsumed youth identity, and upcoming polls in Uttarakhand, Uttar Pradesh, Punjab, Manipur and Goa will test whether street noise translates into organised political preference.

- Akshay Rout writes that while Gen Z street protests have forced parties to engage with youth issues.
- India’s largest youth cohort has not yet consolidated into a predictable electoral bloc, with no discernible youth voting trend in recent Assembly polls in Tamil Nadu.
- Assam.
- West Bengal.
- Keralam and Puducherry.
- About 65% of India’s population is below 35 and roughly 23% of the electorate is aged 18–29, but caste, community and gender identities have largely subsumed youth identity, and upcoming polls in Uttarakhand.
- Uttar Pradesh.
- Punjab.

### Growth with caution (Opinion)

**In short:** Babu Crishna of Bengaluru, referring to commentary on a cautious growth outlook, welcomes 7.8% GDP growth but flags quiet worries beneath the headline: agriculture grew only 3.6%, a below-normal monsoon could hurt rural demand and food prices, inflation is creeping up, and crude prices are rising again.

- Babu Crishna of Bengaluru, referring to commentary on a cautious growth outlook, welcomes 7.8% GDP growth but flags quiet worries beneath the headline: agriculture grew only 3.6%, a below-normal monsoon could hurt rural demand and food prices, inflation is creeping up, and crude prices are rising again.

### For newborns, the answer is hospital plus home (Opinion)

**In short:** Abhay Bang argues that rising institutional deliveries — from 39% in 2005–06 to 90% in 2023–24 — have overcrowded Special Newborn Care Units, contributing to clustered newborn deaths including three killed in an August 24, 2026 fire at Amravati’s government women’s hospital. Citing the Gadchiroli trial’s 62.2% neonatal mortality reduction through home-based neonatal care by trained ASHAs, he advocates a three-part strategy of decongesting units via strengthened home care, improving facility staffing and safety, and ensuring not every newborn who needs care requires a hospital bed.

- Abhay Bang argues that rising institutional deliveries — from 39% in 2005–06 to 90% in 2023–24 — have overcrowded Special Newborn Care Units, contributing to clustered newborn deaths including three killed in an August 24, 2026 fire at Amravati’s government women’s hospital.
- Citing the Gadchiroli trial’s 62.2% neonatal mortality reduction through home-based neonatal care by trained ASHAs, he advocates a three-part strategy of decongesting units via strengthened home care, improving facility staffing and safety, and ensuring not every newborn who needs care requires a hospital bed.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.8_

### A little kindness in a mad, mad world (Opinion)

**In short:** K G Raghavan recalls the 1963 comedy It's a Mad, Mad, Mad, Mad World and his own recent experience at a busy international airport, where strangers helped him with a baggage trolley and heavy luggage after a 20-hour flight, arguing that despite a world often seen as money-driven, small deeds of kindness still exist and must be nurtured, citing Julia A F Carney's Little Things and James Russell Lowell's line that nobleness enkindleth nobleness.

- K G Raghavan recalls the 1963 comedy It's a Mad.
- Mad.
- Mad World and his own recent experience at a busy international airport, where strangers helped him with a baggage trolley and heavy luggage after a 20-hour flight, arguing that despite a world often seen as money-driven, small deeds of kindness still exist and must be nurtured, citing Julia A F Carney's Little Things and James Russell Lowell's line that nobleness enkindleth nobleness.

### Exclusions cannot define electoral roll 'purification' (Opinion)

**In short:** Kamal Laddha of Bengaluru writes that 13 crore names omitted from draft electoral rolls in the first three phases of the SIR reveal a document-heavy EC process biased toward exclusions, and that West Bengal tribunal reinstatements suggest many absent-or-shifted omissions elsewhere may be incorrect; he argues the EC cannot claim purification success on exclusion counts alone unless it onboards every eligible citizen, especially vulnerable and marginalised voters, in the constitutional spirit of universal adult suffrage.

- Kamal Laddha of Bengaluru writes that 13 crore names omitted from draft electoral rolls in the first three phases of the SIR reveal a document-heavy EC process biased toward exclusions, and that West Bengal tribunal reinstatements suggest many absent-or-shifted omissions elsewhere may be incorrect.
- he argues the EC cannot claim purification success on exclusion counts alone unless it onboards every eligible citizen, especially vulnerable and marginalised voters, in the constitutional spirit of universal adult suffrage.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.8_

### Bengaluru's SIR puzzles (Opinion)

**In short:** Kepu Subrahmanya writes that Karnataka's draft SIR roll excluded 1.08 crore of 5.54 crore pre-SIR voters, with 75% in absent or shifted categories, and that in 13 of 28 Bengaluru Urban assembly seats enumerated voters fall below 2024 Lok Sabha votes polled—and in six seats below 2023 assembly votes—an anomaly CM D K Shivakumar wants more enumeration time to resolve. He notes rural constituencies broadly show realistic outcomes with over 85% enrolment in about 150 of 224 seats, while 51 urban seats including Bengaluru and major cities recorded SIR enrolment below the 80.52% state average, correlating with low 2023 turnout, whereas Mangaluru escaped the worst list though with relatively lower enrolment.

- Kepu Subrahmanya writes that Karnataka's draft SIR roll excluded 1.08 crore of 5.54 crore pre-SIR voters, with 75% in absent or shifted categories, and that in 13 of 28 Bengaluru Urban assembly seats enumerated voters fall below 2024 Lok Sabha votes polled—and in six seats below 2023 assembly votes—an anomaly CM D K Shivakumar wants more enumeration time to resolve.

**In short:** Under SPEAK OUT, with an epigraph quoting Amelia Atwater-Rhodes — "Fate has a twisted sense of humour" — Rajya Sabha MP Raghav Chadha says he is a sitting MP from Punjab with voter ID registered in Mohali, yet his name has been classified as "shifted" in the draft electoral rolls.

### Resilience now for future droughts (Opinion)

**In short:** With Karnataka facing a 23% monsoon deficit against India's 14% rainfall gap and about 20 lakh hectares of an 84-lakh-hectare kharif target still unsown, including 15 lakh hectares of cereals, the editorial warns deficient rains, falling groundwater, and crop vulnerability have driven a record 37 lakh crop insurance applications. It urges short-term drinking-water priority in drought-hit rural and urban areas—including Bengaluru, which saw 63% August rainfall shortage and dry borewells—while calling for long-term watershed development, aquifer recharge, rainwater harvesting, crop shifts from water-intensive paddy and sugarcane, and strict Bengaluru enforcement of harvesting, wastewater recycling, and lake revival rather than treating drought as exceptional.

- With Karnataka facing a 23% monsoon deficit against India's 14% rainfall gap and about 20 lakh hectares of an 84-lakh-hectare kharif target still unsown, including 15 lakh hectares of cereals, the editorial warns deficient rains, falling groundwater, and crop vulnerability have driven a record 37 lakh crop insurance applications.
- It urges short-term drinking-water priority in drought-hit rural and urban areas—including Bengaluru, which saw 63% August rainfall shortage and dry borewells—while calling for long-term watershed development, aquifer recharge, rainwater harvesting, crop shifts from water-intensive paddy and sugarcane, and strict Bengaluru enforcement of harvesting, wastewater recycling, and lake revival rather than treating drought as exceptional.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.8_

### Food safety: Message in large print (Opinion)

**In short:** The editorial welcomes FSSAI's Supreme Court-backed proposal for prominent red hexagonal front-of-pack warning labels on foods high in sugar, salt, fat and similar nutrients, arguing small back-of-pack print fails consumers while stage-wise FOPNL starting with warnings for products exceeding limits on two or more components is only a credible start. It urges separate clear markers for every nutrient of concern, strict time-bound implementation after years of delay and industry resistance, and public awareness campaigns, noting India faces rising diabetes, hypertension, obesity and cholesterol linked to unhealthy diets and that the apex court told FSSAI to prioritise citizens' health over multinational interests.

- The editorial welcomes FSSAI's Supreme Court-backed proposal for prominent red hexagonal front-of-pack warning labels on foods high in sugar, salt, fat and similar nutrients, arguing small back-of-pack print fails consumers while stage-wise FOPNL starting with warnings for products exceeding limits on two or more components is only a credible start.
- It urges separate clear markers for every nutrient of concern, strict time-bound implementation after years of delay and industry resistance, and public awareness campaigns, noting India faces rising diabetes, hypertension, obesity and cholesterol linked to unhealthy diets and that the apex court told FSSAI to prioritise citizens' health over multinational interests.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.8_

### Are brands overreacting to manufactured outrage? (Opinion)

**In short:** In a Parley conversation moderated by Sonikka Loganathan, communications leader Mitushi Sharma and Think9 founder Santosh Desai discuss Giva withdrawing a Raksha Bandhan advertisement after netizens criticised Kriti Sanon’s outfit as too revealing, following similar backlashes against Tanishq’s 2020 interfaith ad and Fab India’s 2021 Jashn-e-Riwaaz campaign. Sharma and Desai debate whether brands are reacting from fear, employee safety concerns or commercial caution, and whether such withdrawals reflect manufactured outrage and growing self-censorship rather than genuine cultural sensitivity.

- In a Parley conversation moderated by Sonikka Loganathan, communications leader Mitushi Sharma and Think9 founder Santosh Desai discuss Giva withdrawing a Raksha Bandhan advertisement after netizens criticised Kriti Sanon’s outfit as too revealing, following similar backlashes against Tanishq’s 2020 interfaith ad and Fab India’s 2021 Jashn-e-Riwaaz campaign.
- Sharma and Desai debate whether brands are reacting from fear, employee safety concerns or commercial caution, and whether such withdrawals reflect manufactured outrage and growing self-censorship rather than genuine cultural sensitivity.

### Jealousy is dependency in disguise (Opinion)

**In short:** Acharya Prashant writes that jealousy reveals where self-worth is kept — not in one's own work but in others' praise — and that hating someone praised feels like theft when one was only carrying "borrowed jewellery." He argues moralising or suppressing jealousy only poisons it, while a jealous ego needs others to remain lower and cannot truly admire excellence; building confidence merely upgrades the external supply rather than ending dependence.

- Acharya Prashant writes that jealousy reveals where self-worth is kept — not in one's own work but in others' praise — and that hating someone praised feels like theft when one was only carrying "borrowed jewellery." _Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.9_

### Keeping pace with a succession of climate shock events (Opinion)

**In short:** Meera Srinivasan writes that Nepal’s Rasuwa floods, with over 1,250 lives lost and more than 4,000 still missing, and Sri Lanka’s Cyclone Ditwah highlight unequal vulnerabilities across South Asia, including marginalised Malaiyaha Tamil estate workers in Sri Lanka who remain displaced months later. She notes repeated regional climate shocks from Pakistan’s 2022 floods to Nepal’s 2026 disaster, and cites former diplomat Shyam Saran’s 2009 argument that South Asia is an ecologically interconnected system needing collaborative climate response rather than isolated disaster coverage.

- Meera Srinivasan writes that Nepal’s Rasuwa floods, with over 1,250 lives lost and more than 4,000 still missing, and Sri Lanka’s Cyclone Ditwah highlight unequal vulnerabilities across South Asia, including marginalised Malaiyaha Tamil estate workers in Sri Lanka who remain displaced months later.

### Demolition needs a uniform policy (Opinion)

**In short:** Lakshita Handa writes that on August 12 a Supreme Court bench headed by the Chief Justice considered a petition for a uniform pan-India policy on demolition of unauthorised constructions, noting demolition must go hand in hand with rehabilitation. Citing a 2024 judgment barring punitive demolitions without due process, she points to Delhi's intensified crackdown — since June 1 the Municipal Corporation of Delhi has demolished 681 unauthorised properties and sealed 398 — and argues states should mandate at least 30 days' show-cause notice, personal hearings, restricted use of force, and three-to-six months' rehabilitation for those rendered homeless, especially vulnerable groups.

- Lakshita Handa writes that on August 12 a Supreme Court bench headed by the Chief Justice considered a petition for a uniform pan-India policy on demolition of unauthorised constructions, noting demolition must go hand in hand with rehabilitation.
- Citing a 2024 judgment barring punitive demolitions without due process, she points to Delhi's intensified crackdown — since June 1 the Municipal Corporation of Delhi has demolished 681 unauthorised properties and sealed 398 — and argues states should mandate at least 30 days' show-cause notice, personal hearings, restricted use of force, and three-to-six months' rehabilitation for those rendered homeless, especially vulnerable groups.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.9_

### Punjab: From breadbasket to a crisis of opportunity (Opinion)

**In short:** Lt Gen Bhopinder Singh (retd) writes that Punjab, once India's Green Revolution breadbasket, now faces depleted groundwater from water-intensive paddy, farm indebtedness, industrial stagnation, drug addiction, unemployment and youth leaving "in hoards" abroad because home offers too few possibilities. As the state braces for elections, he argues public discourse is dominated by religion, caste and intra-party power struggles rather than a credible long-term plan for agricultural reform, water conservation, industrial investment and making staying home a credible choice rather than an act of sacrifice.

- Lt Gen Bhopinder Singh (retd) writes that Punjab, once India's Green Revolution breadbasket, now faces depleted groundwater from water-intensive paddy, farm indebtedness, industrial stagnation, drug addiction, unemployment and youth leaving "in hoards" abroad because home offers too few possibilities.

### The charioteer of devotees (Opinion)

**In short:** In the FAITH column, Damal S Ramakrishnan writes that Krishna chose Vasudeva and Devaki as birth parents under Rohini on Ashtami in Avani to vanquish Kamsa, yet remained on earth to attain guru status and reinforce the acharya’s role. After vanquishing Kamsa, marrying Rukmini, protecting Draupadi and delivering the Bhagavad Gita as charioteer to mankind, Krishna studied under sage Sandipini, restored his guru’s son as dakshina, and at Kurukshetra became Gita acharya when Arjuna refused to fight relatives, combining karma, gnana and bhakti yoga in 15 chapters.

- In the FAITH column.

Kaushik writes that Pakistani cricket has become predictably messy, with the Pakistan Cricket Board's unprecedented mid-series move to banish seven players — including former captains Salman Ali Agha and Mohammad Rizwan — along with head coach Sarfaraz Ahmed and bowling coach Umar Gul after heavy defeats in England ranking among its most bizarre reactions.

### Why accessible rehabilitation is the need of the hour in India (Opinion)

**In short:** On World Physiotherapy Day, the authors cite Sidhartha Bandha's father's stroke to show how distant, costly physiotherapy left him with contractures after hospital care saved his life, and note that India has only about 52,000 practising physiotherapists — 0.36 per 10,000 people — with most concentrated in cities. They call for community-based learning, home rehabilitation support, tele-rehabilitation, and rural postings so physiotherapy is treated as lifesaving care rather than an optional add-on.

- On World Physiotherapy Day, the authors cite Sidhartha Bandha's father's stroke to show how distant, costly physiotherapy left him with contractures after hospital care saved his life, and note that India has only about 52,000 practising physiotherapists — 0.36 per 10,000 people — with most concentrated in cities.
- They call for community-based learning, home rehabilitation support, tele-rehabilitation, and rural postings so physiotherapy is treated as lifesaving care rather than an optional add-on.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.18_

### The gap in manufacturing sector GVA (Opinion)

**In short:** Jatinder S. Bedi and R.

- Jatinder S.
- Bedi and R.
- Nagaraj write that National Accounts Statistics puts manufacturing gross value added at ₹38.6 lakh crore for 2023-24 — 14.7% of GDP — but an alternative estimate from Annual Survey of Industries and Annual Survey of Unincorporated Sector Enterprises data totals ₹27.4 lakh crore, a 40.9% gap.
- Even after adding an estimated ₹3.6 lakh crore from residual workers not captured in those surveys, the potential GVA of ₹31.0 lakh crore still falls 24.5% short of the official figure, leaving ₹7.6 lakh crore unexplained unless MCA-21 corporate data and NSO methodologies are opened for independent verification.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.19_
